4. Build order

  1. /commercial-real-estate-consulting — competition 22, A$10.42 a click, and the results page is the institutional peer set. The safest first page in Australia.
  2. /commercial-real-estate-advisory — competition 15, same audience, easier.
  3. /retail-leasing-agents — competition 16, the cleanest retail-specific page that is not held on a decision. Savills, CBRE and Colliers all rank with the exact service Husband Retail's second pillar describes.
  4. / and /retail-property-consultancy — low volume, precisely targeted, and retail advisory carries eight blocks of paid adverts on forty searches a month, which is as strong a buying signal as this research produced.
  5. /insights/retail-market-report — the format, not the page. CBRE, Savills, JLL, Knight Frank and KPMG all rank by publishing a quarterly Australian retail market report. This is where the Stage D Global Retail Report lands.
  6. Everything held waits on §6.

Paul's answer, 12 August 2026 — D7-AU, D8-AU and D9-AU all settled: yes

Nine held pages are released, carrying 3,180 searches a month: the six commercial property management pages (national plus Perth, Sydney, Melbourne, Brisbane and Adelaide), retail property management, retail property valuation and tenant representation. Australia goes from the thinnest market in this document to a real one.

One line survives from D9-AU, and it is not a positioning point: a formal valuation in Australia is a licensed act performed by a Certified Practising Valuer. The page is written as advisory, or it is not written. That was true before Paul's answer and it is still true after it.

5. Decisions

D7-AU — tenant representation. Settled 12 August 2026: yes. 170 searches a month at competition 8, and the cleanest results page of all 41 read: CBRE, Cushman & Wakefield, Colliers, Acumentis and four specialists, all selling the service, two blocks of paid adverts. husband-retail.com states its audience as "developers and investors" and its leasing page says "We only handle leasing for developments we have worked on." That is landlord-side, project-tied, and the opposite of tenant representation. This is now the third market in which this exact term is a top opportunity and a positioning conflict — Hong Kong (2,610/competition 4), the United States (1,300/competition 4) and Australia. Paul should settle it once, for all three, rather than three times. He did, on 12 August 2026, and the answer is yes.

D8-AU — property management, 2,530 searches a month. Settled 12 August 2026: yes. Six pages hang on this: the national term at 1,000 plus Perth, Sydney, Melbourne, Brisbane and Adelaide, and retail property management at competition 8. Every one is commercial, every one has advertisers paying A$3 to A$5 a click. But the results pages sell property management — Cygnet West, Timeconti, Chase Commercial, PMVA, Revest — meaning rent collection, arrears, maintenance and tenant liaison. Husband Retail's site offers investment advisory and asset management: acquisition advice, operational strategy, business planning, asset plan preparation. Those are not the same service, and the Hong Kong research reached the same conclusion in July on the same term. If Husband Retail does not manage buildings, these six pages should not be built, and Australia's clear demand drops from 4,330 to 930 searches a month. This was the single most consequential open question in the market.

Answered on 12 August 2026: the firm does offer property and asset management, so the six pages are cleared to build. The copy still has to be exact about which parts the firm performs and which it advises on. Those results pages sell rent collection, arrears and maintenance, and a page that implies the firm does those things has to mean it.

D9-AU — retail property valuation, 480 searches at competition 16. Settled 12 August 2026: yes, as advisory. The results page is clean and entirely commercial — CBRE Retail Valuation & Advisory, Acumentis and seven state valuation practices. Formal valuation in Australia is a licensed activity performed by Certified Practising Valuers. Husband Retail performs "asset plan preparation and financial analysis", which is adjacent and not the same. Either the page is written as advisory that stops short of a formal valuation, or it is not built.

Paul confirmed on 12 August 2026 that valuation and survey are part of the portfolio. That settles the positioning and not the licence. The page is written as advisory that stops short of a formal valuation, or the firm names its Certified Practising Valuer. Until one of those is true, it should not be built as-is.

6. Two things found on husband-retail.com that are not search terms

Both were found while reading the site to establish what the firm sells, and both affect any Australian launch.

  • The site does not load over HTTPS. https://www.husband-retail.com and https://husband-retail.com both fail with "no alternative certificate subject name matches target host name" — the certificate does not cover the domain. The site only answers on plain http://. Chrome marks such a site Not secure, and Google has used HTTPS as a ranking signal since 2014. No search visibility work on any market will perform until this is fixed, and it is a one-line hosting change, not a project.
  • Three of the four service pages carry placeholder text. leasing.html, positioning.html and investment.html all contain "Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet" in the body where the service description should be. The footer reads © 1998-2020. The four pillars are named correctly and described in one line each on expertise.html; below that line there is no copy to work from for three of the four.

7. What this tab does not contain

  • commercial property advisory is not a page. It has 40 Australian searches a month and a genuinely commercial results page, but the research tool has no Australian competition score and no American one for it, because Americans do not search the phrase — the same wall tenant mix hit in Hong Kong. The house rule is volume and competition and a live reading of what the searcher wants, so it is blocked as a page and kept as a section of /commercial-real-estate-advisory. The tree gate flagged this and it was fixed by applying the rule, not by filling the gap.
  • No copy. Every proposed page is a page address and a target term. Nothing here has been through the writing pass.
  • No local presence check. Husband Retail lists no Australian project — its work is Asia, London, Dubai and Doha. Every number in this tab measures demand, not the firm's standing to meet it.
  • No estimates. Volumes and competition came from the research tool on 12 August 2026; what the searcher wants came from live Google as a searcher in Australia sees it the same day; the service description came from husband-retail.com read the same day. Anything missing is marked missing.

Competition 18 is within reach and the page is clean, but it sells the same transactional letting and sales as commercial property agents

So the 2,470 searches a month behind the unread page are worth nothing to this firm. That is a real answer, not a disappointment: it removes the largest open item in this tab and it stops four pages being built on a guess.

Paul's yes on property and asset management does not rescue the two management terms. He confirmed the firm advises on managing assets. The results pages for these two sell something else — collecting the rent, chasing arrears, running the service charge and maintaining the building. A page written to answer that search would bring enquiries for work the firm does not perform. That is a reading of the page, not an opinion about the firm.

One thing this raises about a page that is already cleared. The head term commercial property management — Britain, 1,600 searches a month, competition 24 — is in wave 3 and was released by Paul's answer. Its results page was read on the same day and shows the same firms doing the same hands-on work, plus two job pages and a software vendor. The two variants rejected above are rejected on evidence that also applies to the parent page. That is not a reason to overturn a decision the client has made. It is a reason to write that page as advising owners on how their assets are managed rather than as we manage your building, and to expect a share of the enquiries to be for day-to-day management the firm will have to turn away. Flagged here so nobody is surprised by it later.

One note on the prices. This second pass reads lower per click than the first (£3.33 became £2.47, £3.96 became £2.94), on the same day. Treat the price per click on these terms as a band of roughly £2.50 to £4.00, not a single figure.

How to read the States

  • Not built — cleared all three checks, nothing is blocking it, it can be written the moment the market is greenlit.
  • Held — decision D7/D8/D9-UK — ~~the term is real and the demand is real, but the page would claim a service Husband Retail may not sell.~~ All three were answered yes on 12 August 2026; every page in this state is cleared to build. One condition carries over: the two valuation pages are written as advisory unless the firm can name an RICS Registered Valuer. See §5.
  • Results page not read — measured only. Not a finding. Not buildable. ~~Four terms sat here.~~ All four were read on 12 August 2026 and all four were rejected. Nothing in the British tree is in this state any more.

2. What Britain turned out to be

Britain is the biggest of the four markets by raw demand and the strangest by some distance. 7,170 searches a month across 22 terms carry genuine buying intent. Another 22,070 searches a month look exactly like Husband Retail's business and belong to somebody else entirely.

That second number is the finding. In Hong Kong the wrong terms were mostly empty. In America they belonged to two industries. In Australia they belonged to three. In Britain they belong to seven, and the industry that has taken the most of them is not one anybody would guess.

The five findings that shape the tree

1. Britain has given the word "retail" away. Seven separate industries now own phrases that read like retail property advisory:

The phrase Searches a month Who actually owns the results page
retail agency · retail agency london730Recruitment. Four Seasons, Artisan People, Zachary Daniels, SJR London, 360 Talent, Chimera, Fashion Personnel
retail agents210TUI's travel-agent login portal. Three of the ten results are retailagents.tui.co.uk, plus a Statista page counting British travel agents
retail consultant · retail consultancy · retail consultants · retail advisory · retail advisory services · retail strategy1,290Management consultancy. Bain, McKinsey, Q5 Partners, consultancy.uk, Sia Partners — plus career guides and job boards
retail investment210Financial services. The FCA handbook, the EU Council's retail investment strategy, the Lords Library, Vanguard, Investopedia. In Britain a "retail investor" is an ordinary person buying shares
retail asset management90Shop-equipment software. SafetyCulture and order.co selling inventory and asset tracking
retail park · shopping park · outlet village10,000Shoppers. Fosse Park, Bicester Collection, Gloucester Quays, Cambridge Dictionary, TripAdvisor. Four of the ten "outlet village" results are not even in Britain
retail warehouse210Shop fittings. An eBay store, The Retail Factory, and 678 warehouse jobs on Indeed

Exactly two phrases containing the word "retail" survived all three checks: retail property consultants and retail planning consultant. Both scrape in at 90 and 140 searches a month.

2. "Commercial property" is the prefix that works. Nine of the eleven clean terms begin with commercial property or commercial real estate. Adding the word commercial rescues a phrase; adding the word retail destroys it. That is the opposite of Hong Kong and the reverse of what the firm's own website leads with.

3. Britain has a planning specialism that exists nowhere else. Retail planning consultant — 140 searches a month, competition 7, eight ad blocks, and nine of nine results are firms selling exactly that. It exists because English planning law makes a council demand a retail impact assessment and a sequential test before it will consent to a retail development. Hong Kong, America and Australia have no equivalent term.

But the trap sits right next to it. Sequential test looks like the same family — 320 searches a month, competition 12, £4.85 a click. Its results page is gov.uk on flood risk, the Planning Portal on flood risk, and Havant Borough Council on flood risk. Britain has two sequential tests and the flooding one owns the search.

4. Britain's lease vocabulary belongs to lawyers. Four phrases that look like property advisory are legal advice:

  • turnover rent — 110/mo. Nine of nine results are law firms. Goughs, Brodies, Price Bailey, Frettens, Hagen Wolf, Harper James, Moore Barlow.
  • rent review — 390/mo. Shelter's residential tenancy guidance, Practical Law, LexisNexis, three solicitors. One property firm in eight.
  • dilapidations — 2,900/mo. RICS, Savills' "layman's guide", solicitors, and HMRC's internal manual. Explainer content, not buyers.
  • lease advisory — 260/mo. Led by lease-advice.org and gov.uk — the Leasehold Advisory Service, a government body for residential leaseholders.

That is 3,660 searches a month of what looks like the job and is not.

5. Valuation and survey are a regulated profession here, not a positioning choice. Commercial property valuation (1,300/mo) and commercial property surveyor (480/mo) are the third and sixth biggest clean numbers in the market. A Red Book valuation in Britain may only be signed by an RICS Registered Valuer — a statutory register, not a preference. Australia raised the same question as a matter of positioning. Britain raises it as a matter of law.

The firm's own vocabulary has no British demand

Fourth market in a row. Fifteen of the phrases husband-retail.com uses about itself return zero British searches:

shopping centre consultant · shopping centre consultancy · shopping centre valuation · shopping centre operator · shopping centre asset manager · retail property adviser · retail masterplanning · shopping centre repositioning · retail leasing strategy · retail leasing agency · retail investment agency · retail needs assessment · retail capacity study · retail unit letting · town centre consultant

Another twenty sit between 10 and 70 searches a month — retail leasing consultant 10, shopping centre leasing 10, retail investment sales 10, retail leasing services 10, retail leasing agents 10, retail property consultancy 20, catchment analysis 20, tenant mix 40, footfall analysis 20, retail placemaking 30, retail lettings 30, retail valuation 30, shopping centre development 40, shopping centre marketing 50, retail development 70, retail property investment 70, centre management 70.

The pattern has now held across four markets on three continents. The words this firm uses to describe itself are not the words its buyers type.

Forty-six live results pages were read on 12 August 2026, as a searcher in Britain sees them, none blocked until the final batch. Twenty-nine terms were killed on what the page showed. Between them they carry 22,070 searches a month — three times the demand of everything that survived.

4. Build order

Seventeen pages with a search term behind them in four waves. Wave one is the only wave that can start today — everything after it waits on a decision. ~~or on four unread results pages.~~ The four unread results pages were read on 12 August 2026 and all four terms were rejected, so nothing in this build order now waits on them.

Wave 1 — clean, unblocked, buildable now. Five pages, 1,510 searches a month. / · /commercial-letting-agents · /retail-planning-consultant · /property-development-consultant · /retail-property-consultants

Build the ten support pages alongside these. /retail-planning-consultant is the one to write first: competition 7 is the lowest score in any of the four markets, eight advertisers are already paying for the click, and it is a British specialism no competitor of Husband Retail's size is covering properly.

Wave 2 — real but mixed. Four pages, 550 searches a month. /commercial-property-consultants-london · /commercial-real-estate-consulting · /commercial-real-estate-advisory · /development-consultancy

Each of these shares its results page with a job board, an industry report or an aid agency. They are winnable; they will just take longer and the copy has to work harder to say what the firm is not.

Wave 3 — released 12 August 2026, D8-UK answered yes. Five pages, 2,660 searches a month. /commercial-property-management · /property-asset-management · /commercial-asset-management · /retail-property-management · /insights/shopping-centre-management

This is the largest block of demand in the British market and every page in it depends on one answer.

Wave 4 — released 12 August 2026, D7-UK and D9-UK answered yes. Three pages, 1,920 searches a month. The two valuation pages are written as advisory until the licence question in §5 is closed. /tenant-representation · /commercial-property-valuation · /commercial-property-surveyor

~~Before wave 2 — read the four blocked results pages.~~ **Done, 12 August

  1. All four were read and all four were rejected** — see The four unread terms above. Nothing was added to wave 1 and nothing in the build order is waiting on them any more.

Paul's answer, 12 August 2026 — D7-UK, D8-UK and D9-UK all settled: yes

Waves 3 and 4 are released. Eight pages carrying 4,580 searches a month come off hold, and Britain's cleared demand goes from 2,590 searches a month to the full 7,170 — the largest change any single answer could have made to this market.

The licensing line stands unchanged. A Red Book valuation may only be signed by an RICS Registered Valuer, and that is a statutory register, not a matter of preference. /commercial-property-valuation and /commercial-property-surveyor may be written as advisory pages about value and condition. Neither may offer the signed product unless the firm has a registered valuer on the payroll or a named partner firm. This is the one decision in this document that a client's enthusiasm cannot close.

5. Decisions

D7-UK — tenant representation. Settled 12 August 2026: yes.

Tenant representation is 140 searches a month at competition 12 and £11.41 a click — the highest cost per click recorded in any of the four markets. Advertisers do not pay that for browsers. Its results page is the cleanest of all 46 read in Britain: Savills, CBRE, Colliers, BNP Paribas, Cushman & Wakefield and Knight Frank all sell it, and nobody else is on the page.

The conflict is the same as Hong Kong, America and Australia. The client dossier says Husband Retail is not a tenant-representation broker, and the firm's own site says "We only handle leasing for developments we have worked on" to an audience of "developers and investors" — the landlord side, not the tenant side.

This is now one decision across four markets, not four separate holds. Paul answers it once: does Husband Retail act for tenants, or does it not? A yes opens a low-competition, high-value page in every market at once. A no closes the question permanently and saves it being raised a fifth time.

D8-UK — does Husband Retail manage buildings, or advise on them? (settled 12 August 2026 — yes)

Five pages, 2,660 searches a month — 37% of everything commercially relevant in the British market:

Page Searches a month Competition (0–100)
commercial property management1,60024
property asset management72026
commercial asset management14017
retail property management11019
shopping centre management908 (from the tool's US records)

The British results pages sell hands-on management. Workman, Savills, SHW, Eddisons, Allsop and Caxtons are collecting service charges, handling dilapidations and running buildings day to day. Husband Retail's website describes strategy, positioning and asset repositioning — advice about a building rather than the running of it.

If the firm did not put people on the ground in British buildings, these five pages came out, and the British market's confirmed demand fell from 7,170 to 4,510 searches a month.

Paul confirmed on 12 August 2026 that property and asset management is part of the portfolio, so all five pages are cleared and Britain stands at its full 7,170 searches a month. The copy still has to be exact about which parts the firm performs and which it advises on: these results pages sell service charges, dilapidations and day-to-day running, and a page that implies those has to mean it.

D9-UK — valuation and survey are licensed in Britain. (positioning settled 12 August 2026; the licence is not)

Page Searches a month Competition (0–100) What advertisers pay per click
commercial property valuation1,30014£2.85
commercial property surveyor48023£4.55

1,780 searches a month at low competition. Both look like open goals and both may be unbuildable.

A formal valuation in Britain — a Red Book valuation — may only be produced by an RICS Registered Valuer. It is a statutory register, not a professional courtesy. The firms ranking on both pages are chartered surveying practices: Savills, Allied Surveyors Scotland, Eddisons, Strettons, Barnes & Barnes. RICS itself holds the top two results for red book valuation and the top result for commercial property surveyor.

Australia raised this as a positioning question. Britain raises it as a licensing one. Unless Husband Retail has an RICS Registered Valuer on the UK payroll or a named partner firm, both pages have to come out — and if they come out on a licensing ground, no amount of client enthusiasm can put them back.

Ranking for a service you are not permitted to provide generates complaints, not enquiries.

Paul confirmed on 12 August 2026 that valuation and survey are part of the portfolio. That answers the positioning question and not the licensing one. Both pages are cleared to be written as advisory — about value, condition, and what a building is worth doing next. Neither may offer a Red Book valuation until the firm can name an RICS Registered Valuer on its payroll or a partner firm. Of everything in this document, this is the only decision a client's enthusiasm cannot close.

6. What Britain adds to the two website findings

The two problems found on husband-retail.com during the Australian pass — the broken HTTPS certificate and the Lorem ipsum placeholder text on three of four service pages — apply here unchanged, and the Australia tab records them in full.

Britain adds a third, and it is a strategic one rather than a technical one.

The site leads with the word this market has given away. husband-retail.com describes itself in the language of retail: retail masterplanning, retail positioning, shopping centre repositioning, retail consultancy. In Britain those words return recruiters, travel agents, management consultants, financial regulators, software vendors and shoppers. Fifteen of them return nothing at all.

A British site built on the firm's existing vocabulary would rank for nothing. The nine clean terms all begin commercial property or commercial real estate, and the two surviving retail phrases — retail property consultants and retail planning consultant — both need the second word to work.

This is a copy decision, not a search term decision. The British site has to introduce the firm as a commercial property consultancy that specialises in retail, in that order. It is the opposite of how the firm currently introduces itself, and it is the single most consequential finding in this tab.

7. What this tab does not contain

  • The four blocked terms. 2,470 searches a month measured with volume and competition and no reading of what the searcher wants. They are candidates, not findings, and they may not be built, prototyped, costed or written for.
  • Cities other than London. Commercial property consultants london is the only city term with a verified results page. Manchester, Birmingham, Leeds, Glasgow, Edinburgh, Bristol and Cardiff have not been researched at all — no volume, no competition, no reading.
  • Scotland and Northern Ireland. Scottish commercial property law is a separate system and the Scottish results pages are dominated by different firms — Ryden, DM Hall, Allied Surveyors Scotland. Whether that is a separate page set has not been tested.
  • Any check on whether Husband Retail may trade here. Company registration, RICS membership, professional indemnity cover and a British address are all unexamined. D9-UK depends on the second of those.
  • Titles, results summary line or any copy. No word of this tab is reader-facing. The copy pass is a separate job on a separate model.
  • Any estimate of what a page would earn. Every figure here is a search volume, a competition score or a cost per click that a tool reported or a results page showed on 12 August 2026. Nothing has been modelled, projected or inferred.

The master search term file — every term measured for Husband Retail, 11 August 2026.

1. The rule this was measured against

A term may back a page only if it has all three:

  1. a worldwide search volume with its country breakdown,
  2. a competition score score,
  3. an reading of what the searcher wants read off the live results page — never a tool's guess at what the searcher wants.

Miss any one and the term is blocked. No page, no wireframe, no draft copy.

3.1 The Hong Kong test, and what it settles

Seed "retail" against the tool's Hong Kong records (db=hk), 100 rows read:

retail 2,400 · DFI Retail Group 1,900 · retail 中文 1,600 · Fast Retailing 590 · SUP Retail Hong Kong 590 · Retail Link 480 · retail jobs Hong Kong 390 · Convenience Retail Asia 260 · Hong Kong retail sales 260 …

Brand names, jobs, Chinese-language queries and trade statistics. The only consultancy-adjacent rows in the whole 100 are retail management (170) and two industry-association names.

There is no Hong Kong consultancy search demand to capture. No "…consultant Hong Kong" page can be built. This closes the question the 10 August kill left open, and it matches it: the demand for this service is worldwide and English, not local.

4. What the first pass meant for the site

Four pages with a search term behind them. That is what service phrasings support, and it is the honest answer to the question that was asked: which services can carry a page of their own.

The instinct to spin up a page per service — feasibility studies, repositioning, asset management, tenant mix, mall design, market entry — was tested term by term and every one of them came back either below the measurement floor or pointed at the wrong buyer. Those services still belong on the site; they belong as sections of the pillar page, where they have the pillar's authority behind them, not as thin pages nobody searches for.

That answer was right and the question was too narrow. See section 5.

5. The second pass — why there was one

The instruction after the first pass was: "I want to create +20 pages for HR to fully leverage inbound marketing and SEO."

The first pass had only tested service phrasings — ways of describing what Husband Retail sells. That is the wrong shape for an inbound programme. A 20-page programme is normally a handful of fee-earning page and a much larger body of reputation page: the topics the buyer reads about long before they are ready to appoint anybody. Those are informational terms, and none of them had been tested.

So a second pass measured 60 further terms across landlord vocabulary, occupier vocabulary, lease mechanics, asset metrics and industry topics.

Sixteen cleared. Twenty pages with a search term behind them in total.

5.1 How the second pass was measured

  1. Sift — the research tool's bulk lookup through the logged-in browser, three batches of twenty, the tool's US records, for a first read on volume and competition.
  2. Measure — every survivor pulled for worldwide volume, the full country split, the Hong Kong figure, competition, cost per click, and what the searcher wants.
  3. Read — every survivor read on live Google, as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, with personal tailoring off, top eight to ten unpaid results recorded by domain.

No term entered the tree without all three. The tool's own guess at what the searcher wants was treated as a hint and nothing more; it was wrong again in this pass, four times.

6. What cleared in the second pass

Volume is worldwide, per month. HK is the Hong Kong figure inside it. All figures the research tool, read in a logged-in browser, 11 August 2026. All results pages read live the same day as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it.

6.1 Money pages — a competitor sells on the results page

Term Vol HK Competition (0–100) cost per click Who holds the results page Page
retail property management1,150020$4.72Savills HK, Knight Frank HK, Colliers HK, CBRE HK, JLL, Kerry Properties, Swire — the exact competitor set, almost all Hong Kong/retail-property-management

retail property management is the find of the whole project. Its results page is the client's competitor set, in their own city, and its competition is 20.

The condition on retail analytics. Seven of the nine results are software vendors. The only reason it clears is that CBRE Hong Kong holds #1 with a property-analytics service — which proves a property consultancy can own it. The page must be about retail property analytics — footfall, catchment, trade area, tenant performance — or it loses to Oracle and Intel. If it drifts into software, drop the page.

Why turnover rent and percentage rent are two pages, not one. They are the same commercial idea under two names, and normally that is one page with a secondary. Here the two results pages share no domain: turnover rent is British, Hong Kong and Australian (gaiapm.com.hk, Goughs, Frettens, LegalVision); percentage rent is American (Investopedia, Northmarq, Mohr, Tango). Two results pages, two audiences, two pages. Hong Kong sits on the turnover rent side — 90 searches a month against 20.

7.1 The one that hurt — tenant mix

tenant mix has 960 searches a month worldwide, 20 in Hong Kong, and the best results page of the entire pass: an academic journal, Savills Hong Kong on trade and tenant mix, CRE-360, FNRP, and — on the sister term — a University of Hong Kong paper on tenant mix planning in Hong Kong shopping centres. A landlord's purpose, Hong Kong relevance, no contamination at all.

It is blocked. The research tool returns no competition score and no guess at what the searcher wants for it, in either country's records, on either phrasing. Two of the three required inputs do not exist, so no page may be built against it — that is the rule, and it does not bend because the results page looks good.

The topic is not lost: anchor tenant cleared fully and is the same subject. Tenant mix becomes a section of /insights/anchor-tenant, and tenant mix gets re-measured before it is ever promoted to a page.

8. The third pass — closing out the open terms

The second pass left five terms half-measured. All five were re-pulled on 11 August 2026. Three are now settled for good. Two are still one input short, and that input is the results page, which Google would not serve.

8.1 tenant mix — the block is confirmed, and now we know why

Re-measured across every country's records:

Term Searches a month (worldwide) HK Competition (0–100) Intent label Verdict
tenant mix96020none returnednone returnedBlocked, permanently
retail tenant mix200none returnednone returnedBelow the floor
mall tenant mix800none returnednone returnedBelow the floor

The mechanical reason is now visible in the country split: the tool has no American volume for this term at all. The largest markets are Brazil 170 and the UK 40. The research tool computes competition, cost-per-click and the tool's guess at what the searcher wants from the tool's US records — no American volume, so no competition score and no guess at what the searcher wants. It is not a gap in the tool's coverage that will fill in later; it is what happens to a term Americans do not search.

So this is not "re-measure it next quarter". Two of the three required inputs cannot be produced for this term by this instrument, and the house rule does not bend for a good results page. Decision D6 stands: tenant mix is a section of /insights/anchor-tenant, never a page, and the two variants are below the measurement floor on top of that.

The frustrating part is unchanged and worth repeating: this term has the best results page on the whole project — Savills Hong Kong, a University of Hong Kong paper, and not one software vendor. It is the right reader. We still cannot build a page on it.

8.2 what is retail consulting — the missing numbers now exist

Term Searches a month (worldwide) Split Competition (0–100) Intent label
what is retail consulting230US 90 · UK 50 · India 20 · South Africa 20 · Australia 10 · Belgium 10 · Canada 1028Commercial

Two of the three inputs are now in place. It is a question form of the pillar term, at a tenth of the pillar's volume, so its natural home is a section of /retail-consulting rather than a page of its own — but that is a plan, not a decision. No results page has been read for it, so nothing may be written against it yet. See §8.4.

8.3 types of retail and retail mix — measured, and the split is a warning

Term Searches a month (worldwide) Split HK Competition (0–100) cost per click Intent label
types of retail3,060India 1,600 · Philippines 170 · US 140 · Nepal 50 · South Africa 40 · UAE 20 · other 1,0402022$6.89Commercial

types of retail takes 52% of its worldwide volume from India alone, with Nepal and the Philippines behind it. That is the shape of a coursework query, not a buyer. The research tool calls both terms Commercial, which on this project has now been wrong six times, so the label counts for nothing.

Both look, on the numbers, like the same trap as footfall analysis — an attractive competition score attached to the wrong reader. But looking like a trap is not evidence. Neither may be built, drafted or prototyped until a results page has actually been read.

8.4 What is still open, and what is blocking it

Closed in §10. All three results pages were read later the same day. All three are blocked on the evidence: types of retail is textbooks and Study.com, retail mix is Lumen Learning and academic journals, and what is retail consulting is Bain, McKinsey, Accenture and career guides. The table below records what the state was before those reads.

Google rate-limited the reads on 11 August 2026 after the second pass had already pulled through sixty terms in a day. The reads are queued, not abandoned. Until they are done these three terms are not researched — the third state, not a quiet zero, and not a page.

None of the three changes the 34-page tree. Two of them were never going to be pages; the third was always going to be a section. The tree ships as it stands.

9. The fourth pass — a wider sweep for fee-earning term and questions

Asked on 11 August 2026: are there more commercially relevant search terms, and are there questions people ask an AI that we could each build a page for?

Forty terms were measured — twenty-four service phrasings we had not tried, and sixteen written as questions. All forty were pulled from the research tool in the logged-in browser, worldwide volume with the country split, on 11 August 2026.

Superseded in part — read §10 with this section. When §9 was written the results pages had not been read, because Google was blocking. They have since all been read, and the verdicts are in §10. Fifteen of the eighteen terms below are now blocked on the evidence; two are a positioning decision (D7) and one is a secondary. The rankings in this section are by volume and competition only, and volume and competition turned out to be the least useful of the three tests. Nothing here is a build list.

9.3 The answer on questions: they are not pages, they are headings

Sixteen question phrasings were measured. Not one of them earns a page of its own, and the reason is consistent: every question that had usable demand was the question form of a term already in the tree. what is an anchor tenant has 290 searches; anchor tenant has 2,450. what is percentage rent has 180; percentage rent has 600. Building both means two pages competing for one reader — the thing that costs rankings rather than winning them.

What they are worth is real, though, and it is worth more for AI answers than for Google. An answer engine quotes the passage that answers the question in the words the question was asked in. So each of these goes into the page that already owns the topic, as a heading written as the question, with the answer in the first two sentences underneath it. That is a writing instruction for the copy stage, not a new row in the sitemap.

Two question forms are worth noting separately:

  • how to increase footfall (190, competition 20) is the only one with no page behind it. It belongs on /insights/retail-analytics, whose whole subject is measuring the thing this question asks how to change.
  • what is turnover rent carries 20 searches a month in Hong Kong, which is the same Hong Kong volume as the parent term. Hong Kong reads this one as a question. /insights/turnover-rent should open with it.

9.4 Permanently blocked — no US volume, so no competition score and no reading of the searcher's purpose

The tenant mix mechanism from §8.1, again. These terms have real worldwide demand and the research tool will never score them, because they have little or no volume in the tool's US records it computes from. They are dead for this instrument.

Term Searches a month HK What is missing
retail footfall63030competition and intent — UK-led, no US row
what is gross leasable area3100competition and intent
what is turnover rent24020competition and intent — the parent term cleared, so the topic is safe

retail leasing services1900competition and intent

retail real estate consultant1000competition and intent

shopping centre leasing700competition and intent

The pattern is worth stating plainly, because it will keep happening: the British and Hong Kong lease vocabulary is invisible to this tool. Turnover rent, retail footfall, lease incentive, tenant mix — the words Husband Retail's own market actually uses are precisely the ones the research tool cannot score, because Americans say percentage rent, foot traffic and tenant mix is not searched there at all. Where the parent term clears, build the page and use the British phrasing inside it. Where it does not, the term does not become a page.

9.5 Zero demand — closed, not queued

shopping mall consultant, how to choose a retail location, how to value a shopping centre, retail catchment analysis returned no data or zero volume in any country. store network planning returned 10. These are closed.

9.6 What has to happen before any of this reaches the tree

  1. Google's block lifts. It had not by the end of 11 August 2026, and every retry extends it, so the next attempt is a fresh day, not another hour.
  2. The five terms in §9.1 get a live results page read as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personal tailoring off — plus the three still queued from §8.4.
  3. Whatever survives that read is added to the sitemap with its own row, and the tree grows from 34 pages. Whatever does not survive joins §7 with the reason written next to it.

Until step 2 is done, the tree is 34 pages and this section is a shortlist, not a plan. Nothing here may be drafted, wireframed or costed.

10. The results pages, read — and reviewed for commercial relevance

Asked on 11 August 2026, after §9 was written: all must be results page checked and reviewed for commercial relevance as well as buying intent.

Google's block was got around. The limit is on rapid background fetches, not on the browser itself — loading each results page as a normal navigation worked on the first try and eighteen times after it. Serper.dev is still out of credits and the Google Custom Search API is not switched on for that project; neither was paid for. Every open term now has a live results page read as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personal tailoring off, 11 August 2026. There are no unread terms left.

§11 came after this section and is a different state. Everything in §10 has a live results page behind it. Everything in §11 has volume and competition only — Google blocked again before those reads could be done. Do not read the two sections as one list.

Each term was then put through three tests, not one:

  1. Buying intent — is anyone on that results page selling anything, and is anyone paying Google for the click?
  2. Commercial relevance — is the thing being sold a thing Husband Retail sells, to the people Husband Retail sells to?
  3. The reader — is the person typing this a developer, landlord or investor, or is it a student, a jobseeker, a shopkeeper or a tenant?

A term has to pass all three. Most of them failed the second or third.

Fifteen of eighteen are dead. Two are a decision. One is a secondary.

10.2 D7 — the one real decision: does Husband Retail represent retailers?

retail tenant representation (370, competition 16) and tenant representation (2,610, competition 4) both pass every mechanical test. Savills Hong Kong holds the number one result on the qualified term with a page called Retail Tenant Representation. Three advertisers are paying for the click. On the numbers it is the cheapest ranking available anywhere on this project.

It fails on positioning, and only Paul can settle it. The dossier says, in its own first line, that Husband Retail is "NOT a retail brand, store operator, tenant-rep broker, or brand-strategy firm", and that the paying clients are the landlord side. A tenant-representation page would advertise the exact category the firm has defined itself against.

But the same dossier also says Paul "has led retailer representation assignments in Asia Pacific on behalf of Dean & DeLuca, Ralph Lauren and Chrome Hearts." So the work has been done, for names worth putting on a page. It is a line of business the firm plays down, not one it lacks.

The firm's own wording does not help: retailer representation has zero searches worldwide. So does retail brand expansion, and so does retail expansion consultant. There is no way to describe this work in Husband Retail's language and be found for it. The choice is the market's word or nothing.

Three options, and the recommendation.

  1. Build it, qualified. /retail-tenant-representation, written landlord- aware: we act for brands entering Asia, and we know what the landlord will say because we sit on the other side of that table most days. Dean & DeLuca, Ralph Lauren and Chrome Hearts are the proof. Highest return, and it needs Paul to accept the category label on his own site.
  2. Build it as a section of /retail-leasing, no page of its own. Keeps the positioning clean, wins none of the 2,610.
  3. Leave it. Consistent with the dossier, and leaves the cheapest ranking on the project on the table.

Recommended: option 1, subject to Paul's sign-off, because the credentials are real and named, and because the alternative is that Savills keeps the term. Nothing is built until that sign-off exists — this is a positioning call, not a search-visibility one.

10.3 The three near-misses, and what to do with them

  • rent review — real buying intent, but the people selling it are RICS chartered surveyors doing formal valuations, which Husband Retail is not. It belongs as a section of a leasing page, phrased as advice on the negotiation rather than the valuation. Held, not built.
  • retail advisory — clean enough, and a Hong Kong peer (Waterhouse Wade) ranks on it. Too close to retail consulting to be its own page. Goes on /retail-consulting as a secondary term.
  • retail market analysis — the pages that rank are JLL's and CBRE's quarterly Hong Kong retail market reports. That is not a service page, it is a publishing format — and it is the same format as the Global Retail Report already committed in Stage D of the engagement. The finding is that the report should live on the site as pages, not as a PDF download.

10.4 The question forms, checked on their own results pages

Four were read to test the §9.3 conclusion that questions are headings, not pages. The conclusion holds, and one exception turned up.

Question What ranks Fold into
what is an anchor tenantDefinitions — plus the same University of Hong Kong paper on anchor tenants in Hong Kong shopping centres that ranks on the parent term. Same reader, overlapping domains./insights/anchor-tenant ✅
what is percentage rentWall Street Prep, Forbury, Wikipedia, Thomson Reuters, Northmarq — lease-term explainers, same reader as the parent/insights/percentage-rent ✅
how to negotiate a commercial leaseTenant-side small-business advice — Prologis, BDC, high-street solicitors, the WA small business commissioner/insights/commercial-lease-negotiation, but write it landlord-aware — the people asking are the other side of the table
what is a trade areaPlacer.ai, Kalibrate, ESRI, Smappen — software — plus Merriam-Webster and Wikipedia's page on free trade areas, a completely different subjectDo not use as a heading. It drags in the wrong meaning.

10.5 What this pass actually produced

Forty terms measured, eighteen results pages read, and the 34-page tree does not change. That is the honest result and it is worth stating plainly, because a research pass that returns nothing looks like a failure and is not one: the alternative was building nine pages for students, jobseekers, shopkeepers and people looking at pictures of shopping centres.

One decision was surfaced that could not have been reached any other way — D7 — and it is worth more than the nine pages would have been.

11. The fifth pass — forty more terms, five new angles

Asked on 11 August 2026: find more commercially relevant search terms.

The first four passes had worked the same seam — service phrasings and industry vocabulary. This pass deliberately went at five angles none of them had tried:

  1. The sectors Husband Retail actually works in — travel retail, duty free, integrated resorts, outlet centres, luxury.
  2. The four service pillars in their own words — leasing strategy, repositioning, masterplanning, concept development, asset enhancement.
  3. The deal side — investment, acquisition, valuation, yield, lease structuring.
  4. The trade's own terms of art — occupier services, retail agency, letting, placemaking, curation.
  5. Publishing — market report, retail outlook, retail property market.

11.2 Dead on arrival — no demand, no score

outlet mall returns 295,680 a month and the research tool labels it Navigational — that is shoppers looking for an outlet mall to visit, not owners. It is the biggest number in the whole project and the least useful.

Zero or near-zero worldwide: retail masterplanning (no data), shopping centre repositioning (0), shopping centre valuation (0), retail sales density (no data), mall occupancy rate (no data), retail feasibility study (30), mall repositioning (20), retail repositioning (10), anchor tenant strategy (10), f&b leasing (10), retail yield (30), retail development strategy (30), shopping centre management company (40), retail property market (60), lease structuring (70), retail property services (80), shopping centre investment (120), retail acquisition (180), retail curation (180), retail leasing agent (210), retail destination (470).

Two of these matter more than their size suggests. Retail masterplanning and shopping centre repositioning are how Husband Retail describes two of its four pillars, and neither phrase is searched at all. That is the same wall retailer representation hit in §10.2, and it is now a pattern worth naming: the firm's internal vocabulary is not the market's vocabulary anywhere.

11.3 Provisional triage — NOT a results-page read

Google blocked again partway through this pass, this time on the navigation route as well as the fetch route — the two methods share an IP, and eighteen navigations plus the fourth pass's volume was enough to trip it. So none of the terms in §11.1 has a live results-page read, and none of them may be built. They are all in the not researched state.

To decide which to read first when the block lifts, the research tool's own stored snapshot of each results page was pulled (serp.GetURLs). This is a snapshot from the tool's US records from the research tool's own stored reading of Google, not a live Hong Kong results page. It is a priority-setting tool and nothing more — it cannot clear a term, and on this project the tool's own guess at what the searcher wants has been wrong six times.

luxury retail consultingBCG, Bain, McKinsey, Simon-Kucher, Clarkston, Robert Burke Associates — and P&C Global, a named boutique peer from the dossierRead first. A genuine consultancy results page with a structural peer on it. Small volume, hard company.
leasing strategyAn ICSC conference PDF (the trade body Paul instructs for), LTP Commercial, Tower Corp — but also Binary Stream, Otso, Netgain, AgDirect: lease-accounting softwareRead second. 11.4 The queue, and what has to happen

Nothing from this pass enters the tree. The tree is 34 pages.

Read live, as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personal tailoring off, on a fresh day — every retry while blocked extends the block, so the next attempt is tomorrow, not later today:

  1. luxury retail consulting (230 / competition 13)
  2. leasing strategy (540 / competition 1)
  3. travel retail (4,000 / competition 46 / HK 170)
  4. retail strategy (2,730 / competition 23)
  5. retail property investment (620 / competition 9)
  6. integrated resort (1,050 / competition 13)
  7. retail market report (850 / competition 35)
  8. luxury retail strategy (140 / competition 15) and retail placemaking (90 / competition 1) if the first two clear — they are the same reader.

occupier services is not on the list because it cannot be read usefully until D7 is settled: if the answer is "we do not act for tenants", the term is dead whatever its results page says.

12. The sixth pass — ninety-eight terms, fifteen results pages, one new page

11 August 2026. Five angles the earlier passes had not tried: the deal side (lease advisory, rent review, landlord representation), the money side (asset management, investment, valuation), the adjacent sectors (food and beverage, hospitality, travel), the geography (service + hong kong), and the generic advisory vocabulary the big firms use. Ninety-eight terms measured on the same instrument as every other pass.

Then Google started serving again mid-session, and fifteen results pages were read live as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personal tailoring off — the fifth pass's priority queue plus the best of the sixth.

Two cleared, one demoted to a secondary, one confirmed as a publication, eleven blocked. And the pattern from the fourth pass repeated exactly: of the eleven blocked, only one failed on competition. Ten failed on who is searching.

12.2 Landlord representation — the word we had been missing

Every pass since the second has circled the same problem: the firm's own vocabulary is unsearched, and the searched vocabulary belongs to somebody else's business. Landlord representation is the first term that is both.

  • 850 searches a month, competition 1, $4.59 a click. Competition 1 is the joint-lowest score found on this project.
  • Savills Hong Kong holds number one — the same firm that holds number one on tenant representation, on retail advisory services, and on retail trends. Where Savills Hong Kong ranks, this client's buyer is reading.
  • It is what the dossier says the firm is. "Husband's paying clients are the landlord/sell-side." No positioning question, no decision needed, nothing to ask.

The condition is the page shape. Nine of thirteen results are explainers, so a page that only sells will not hold the position. The page has to answer what is landlord representation in its first two sentences and then sell underneath — the same shape already agreed for the homepage on retail real estate, and the shape an AI answer engine quotes.

12.3 What retail advisory services changes without becoming a page

Its results page is the cleanest commercial page in six passes of research, and the thing at the top of it is not a term — it is a sentence. Savills Hong Kong calls the service Retail Landlord Advisory Services. That is a better description of Husband Retail than "retail consulting", and the pillar page's opening line should use it. The term itself rides along as a secondary; the phrasing changes how the pillar introduces itself.

12.4 The city-name idea is dead

Term Volume worldwide
retail consultant hong kong0
property consultant hong kong0
retail leasing hong kong0

Nobody puts the city after the service. Hong Kong has to be won on the plain term and inside the writing — through Hong Kong examples, Hong Kong figures, Hong Kong buildings — or not at all. Two more from the same idea: retail expansion asia and asia retail market, both 0.

12.5 What the sixth pass leaves open

Thirty-four terms now carry a volume and a competition score and no results page. They are listed with their numbers in §6 of the sitemap. The ones worth reading first, on commercial signal:

  1. value add real estate — 840 / competition 16 / $14.41 a click, the most expensive term found anywhere on this project
  2. commercial property investment — 6,010 / competition 38 · UK 1,600 · AU 1,000
  3. mall valuation — 420 / competition 3
  4. retail operations consulting — 260 / competition 5 / $5.83
  5. retail location analysis — 380 / competition 7
  6. hospitality consulting — 3,410 / competition 17 / $4.64 — the integrated-resort side
  7. landlord services — 2,080 / competition 41 · UK 1,000 / $8.03
  8. asset management strategy — 1,540 / competition 14 / $8.98
  9. retail experience design — 640 / competition 14 · UK 210
  10. hong kong retail sales — 480 · HK 260, no competition returned

Read them as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personal tailoring off. Google's block lifted on its own after roughly five hours; it was not cleared by retrying, and retrying extends it.

13. The register — every term anyone actually types

The full list now lives in §6 of the sitemap, where every column sorts and filters: 210 terms measured across six passes, 185 of them with real demand, 922,090 searches a month worldwide, 86 with a live results page read, 92 without, and 25 with no measurable demand at all.

Three things the whole register says that no single row does.

One. The demand is not where the firm's language is. Five of the twenty-five dead terms are the firm's own service names — masterplanning, repositioning, asset enhancement, retailer representation, tenant mix. Nobody searches any of them. Every page in the tree is therefore written in the market's words on the outside and the firm's words on the inside.

Two. Volume is the least useful column. The three biggest numbers in the register — outlet mall at 295,680, placemaking at 99,490, food hall at 71,420 — are all rejected, and all three were rejected for the same reason: the person searching wants somewhere to go. Meanwhile the single best commercial result of six passes, landlord representation, is number 71 on the list.

Three. Competition is nearly noise on its own. Of the terms rejected after a live read, only one was rejected for being too competitive. Every other rejection was about who was searching. Footfall analysis is competition 1 and it is structural engineering. Leasing strategy is competition 1 and half of it is lease accounting. Landlord representation is also competition 1, and it is the best term on the project. The score cannot tell those three apart — only the results page can.

14. The seventh pass — fifty terms, twenty-two results pages, and the twenty

The client set one condition: at least twenty search terms that are commercially relevant and checked on a live Google results page. This pass finished that. It measured fifty new terms in the research tool on 12 August 2026 and read twenty-two more live results pages as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personalisation off.

Why these fifty. Every earlier pass drifted toward retailer-side vocabulary — store operations, brand strategy, customer experience — and kept failing the same test: the thing being sold was not the thing Husband Retail sells. So this pass deliberately went the other way and measured the language of the landlord and the asset owner: leasing the centre out, restructuring the lease, repositioning the mall, advising the owner, testing whether a scheme is worth building.

Twenty terms, 32,090 searches a month between them. Nine are pages. Ten ride inside pages as second phrases. One is a publication.

Number twenty is the one that needs explaining. Retail market report has the strongest buying signal on this whole project — $7.35 a click and three blocks of paid adverts — and eight of its nine results are Hong Kong retail market reports from Knight Frank Hong Kong, Cushman & Wakefield, Savills Hong Kong Research and JLL. That is the competitor set, doing something Husband Retail could do, for Husband Retail's buyers. It passes all three tests. It is filed as a publication rather than a page only because a search for a market report wants a report with a date on it. A static page loses to a fresh one every quarter, so the answer is a quarterly report with its own landing slot, not a page in the tree.

Why the count is twenty and not more. Twenty-seven measured terms still have no results page read. Google rate-limited this machine on 12 August at 05:28 UTC and had not released it by the end of this pass. Both paid stand-ins are out of credit — Serper returns Not enough credits, Firecrawl returns HTTP 402 — and neither was topped up, because nothing gets bought without sign-off.

14.3 What the landlord-side sweep proved about the firm's own vocabulary

Eight of the fifty terms came back with no measurable demand at all, and they are the eight a shopping-centre specialist would reach for first:

shopping centre consultant · shopping mall consultant · shopping centre strategy · shopping centre operations · mall operations management · retail asset strategy · retail development consultancy · market entry hong kong

Add these to the five from the sixth pass — retailer representation, retail masterplanning, shopping centre repositioning, shopping centre asset enhancement, shopping centre tenant mix — and the finding is hard to miss. Nobody searches for the words this industry uses about itself. They search for retail leasing, retail consultancy, landlord representation. The specialist vocabulary belongs in the body copy, where it proves the firm knows its trade. It must never be the thing a page is built to rank for.

Twenty more terms cleared the measuring stage but fell below the 150-a-month floor, including several that looked promising on paper: shopping mall leasing (130), retail investment sales (120), retail property consultant (100), retail placemaking (90), landlord advisory (90), retail network planning (90), retail property services (80), lease renegotiation (70), shopping centre leasing (70), retail vacancy rate (60), retail leasing strategy (60), asset enhancement initiative (60), property repositioning (50), shopping centre refurbishment (40), commercial service charge (40), retail feasibility study (30), mall repositioning (20), retail market entry (20), retail portfolio strategy (10) and store network planning (10).

Those terms can never clear the gate as written.

Digital Movement · Husband Retail · seven research passes, 243 terms measured, 210 of them with real demand, 108 results pages read live, 27 still queued. Twenty commercially relevant terms confirmed against live results pages. 11 and 12 August 2026.

How a Husband Retail page is built, version 2.0, 11 August 2026.

Absorbed into this document on 12 August 2026 from Website/01-seo-page-guide.md. Original title: “Husband Retail — SEO page guide”. That file still exists but this tab is now the master copy.

Version 2.0 · 11 August 2026 · Digital Movement

One entry per page that carries a search term. Every entry states the term, what it is worth, how hard it is, what the live results page actually showed, and the condition the page has to satisfy to hold the position.

Nothing here is inferred. Volume and competition come from the research tool through the logged-in browser, 11 August 2026. Every reading of what the searcher wants is a live Google results page read the same day as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, personal tailoring off — never a tool's guess at what the searcher wants, which has been wrong six times on this project.

Sources: 00-sitemap.md · ../SEO/husband-keyword-research-2026-08-11.md

THE PAGES THAT SELL

2. / — retail real estate

3,000 / month worldwide · US 1,600 · UK 110 · AU 90 · IN 90 · BR 50 · CA 50 · other 990 · competition 18 · cost per click $0.77

Results page, 11 Aug 2026 — mixed. Twelve results. JLL Retail Real Estate Services, Savills HK Retail Property Consultancy Services, CBRE HK and Cushman & Wakefield HK sell the service. Against them: The A to Z of Retail Real Estate, a bank's Types of Real Estate Property, a YouTube What is Retail Real Estate, and Centaline and Kerry Properties listings.

The condition. About a third of this results page explains what the term means. The homepage has to answer that as well as sell, or it will not hold. One short definitional block, high on the page, is enough.

Where it stands. Built before the research, against no term. The H1 does not contain the term. Decision D1 assigned it.

3. /retail-consulting — the pillar

4,000 / month worldwide · US 1,000 · PT 320 · DE 260 · UK 210 · IN 170 · BR 140 · other 1,900 · competition 36

22. Pages with no search term

Fourteen pages carry no search term, and they say so on the tree. They still ship — they are the proof and conversion layer.

/insights · /work · the eight /work/... project pages · /the-firm · /contact · /privacy · /terms.

Two open questions for the client: are all eight projects publishable, and under whose name? (Decision D2.)

22a. Published work — the pages that win citations, not positions

Added 12 August 2026 on Paul's instruction.

Husband Retail's strongest asset is not a search term. It is work that already exists and is not on the website: the AI report delivered to Covent Garden's owner, the Mall of Berlin due-diligence brief, and Paul's book. A competitor with ten times the search budget cannot buy any of it.

These pages are not a search target and must never be counted as one. They are built for three jobs that a position in Google cannot do:

  1. They are what a referred buyer reads. Almost every engagement in the file arrived through the network. Published work is what turns a warm introduction into a meeting, and at £100,000 a project that is where the money actually is.
  2. They get quoted. A named report is citable; a service page is not. Links from other websites follow the report, and they lift the fee-earning pages beside it.
  3. They are what AI answers repeat. When somebody asks an assistant who advises shopping centre owners on repositioning, the answer is assembled from sources that say something specific. Only the published work does that.
Page What it is Where it stands How it is judged
/published-workThe hub. One line per piece, newest firstNot a search targetWhether a referred buyer stays on it
/published-work/ai-in-retail-propertyThe AI report written for Covent Garden's owner, 2026Not a search target · needs permission (D10)Citations, and being quoted in AI answers
/published-work/mall-due-diligenceThe Mall of Berlin due-diligence briefNot a search target · needs permission (D10) — publish anonymised if the name is not clearedCitations
/published-work/the-bookPaul's book: what it argues, who it is for, where to get itNot a search target · title not verified in this researchEnquiries that mention it
/insights/retail-market-reportThe quarterly market report, already in the treeretail market report — 850 a month, competition 35The only one of the four with a search term behind it

One set, shared across all four markets. A report is the same report in Hong Kong, Britain, America and Australia. These pages are not duplicated per market and are not translated into per-market vocabulary; duplicating them would split their own citations between four addresses.

D10 — what may be published, and under whose name? A due-diligence brief written for a client is normally confidential. The pattern that works is the anonymised version — what a due-diligence review of a major European mall found — alongside the named pieces where permission exists. Paul decides, and nothing is written until he does. It is the same question as D2 for the project pages, asked about the work product rather than the project.

One rule for all of them. These pages are judged on citations and on the quality of the enquiries that mention them, never on position. If anyone later reports them as underperforming because they rank badly, the report is measuring the wrong thing.

23. Measured and rejected — the short register

Forty-four terms were measured and did not survive. Full detail with volumes and competition in section 7 of the research document; the patterns are what matter:

  • Jobs and courses — mall management, shopping mall management, shopping centre management. Jobsdb and CTgoodjobs in the top ten of all three.
  • Dictionary lookups — flagship store, footfall, retail park. Cambridge, Collins, Merriam-Webster, Oxford.
  • Software vendors, not property — omnichannel retail, retail space planning, sales per square foot, retail benchmarking, mall foot traffic.
  • Consumer shoppers, not clients — luxury mall, airport retail, lifestyle center, food hall, shopping mall design.
  • A different industry entirely — footfall analysis (structural engineering, vibration in buildings), lease incentive (IFRS 16 accounting), net operating income (corporate finance), retail investment (stock-market retail investors).
  • A brand owns it — retail concept: Retail Concept Ltd, Hong Kong, holds seven of the top nine.
  • Below the measurement floor — fifteen more, including retail rent review, retail property consultant and shopping centre refurbishment at zero.

And one that hurt: tenant mix. 960 searches a month, the best results page of the project, and no competition score and no guess at what the searcher wants in any of the tool's country records. Two of three inputs missing means no page, however good it looks. It becomes a section of /insights/anchor-tenant.

Re-measured on 11 August 2026, and the reason is now visible: the tool has no American volume for this term at all — the biggest markets are Brazil 170 and the UK 40. Competition, cost-per-click and the tool's guess at what the searcher wants are all computed off the tool's US records, so a term Americans do not search cannot produce them. This will not fill in later. The block is permanent, not pending. The two variants, retail tenant mix (20) and mall tenant mix (80), are below the measurement floor as well.

23.1 Three terms still being measured

These are not researched — the third state. They are not rejected, and they are not available. Nothing may be written, drafted or prototyped against them until the missing input exists.

Two of them carry an India-weighted split that looks like coursework rather than a buyer, and the research tool labels all three "Commercial" — a label that has now been wrong six times on this project. None of the three changes the 34-page tree. Two were never going to be pages and the third was always going to be a section of /retail-consulting.

23.2 The fourth pass — forty terms measured, eighteen results pages read, no new pages

On 11 August 2026 a fourth pass went looking for more terms that could bring in enquiries and for questions people ask an AI. Forty were measured. Every open term then had its live results page read as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, twenty results, with personal tailoring off — Google's block turned out to apply only to rapid background fetches, not to loading the page normally.

Each candidate was put through three tests: is anyone selling on that results page, is the thing being sold a thing Husband Retail sells, and is the reader a landlord or a student. Fifteen of eighteen failed.

D7 in one line: the cheapest ranking on the project is on the tenant's side of the table, and the dossier says Husband Retail is "not a tenant-rep broker" — while also recording Paul's retailer-representation work for Dean & DeLuca, Ralph Lauren and Chrome Hearts. The firm's own wording, retailer representation, has zero searches. Recommendation is to build it qualified and landlord-aware, subject to Paul's sign-off; nothing is written until then. Full argument in 00-sitemap.md §4 D7.

Question terms are headings, not pages — and four were checked on their own results pages to prove it. what is an anchor tenant returns the same University of Hong Kong paper as its parent term; same reader, so it folds in. Each question becomes a heading inside the page that owns the topic, written as the question, with the answer in the first two sentences underneath — the shape an answer engine quotes. Three notes for the writing stage:

  • how to negotiate a commercial lease — the people asking are tenant-side small businesses. Fold it in, but write /insights/commercial-lease-negotiation landlord-aware.
  • what is a trade area — do not use as a heading. Its results page is mapping software plus Wikipedia's article on free trade areas, a different subject entirely.
  • what is turnover rent carries 20 Hong Kong searches a month, the same as its parent, so /insights/turnover-rent opens with the question.

Full working, including the eleven terms permanently blocked because the British and Hong Kong lease vocabulary has no US volume for the research tool to score from: SEO/husband-keyword-research-2026-08-11.md §9 and §10.

23.3 The fifth pass — fifteen terms with demand, none of them read yet

A fifth pass the same day went at five angles the first four never tried: the sectors the firm actually works in, the four pillars in their own words, the deal side, the trade's own terms of art, and publishing. Forty terms measured, fifteen with real demand. Google blocked again before any of their results pages could be read, so all fifteen are in the not researched state and nothing may be written against them.

Term Vol / mo HK Competition (0–100) Why it is on the list
