Read order when the block lifts: luxury retail consulting, leasing strategy, travel retail, then the rest. The research tool's stored snapshot suggests leasing strategy may be half lease-accounting software — the same trap that killed footfall analysis — so confirm it before planning anything on the competition score alone.

Two findings from this pass that produced no page but change how we write. First, the firm's own vocabulary is not the market's vocabulary anywhere: retail masterplanning and shopping centre repositioning name two of the four pillars and neither is searched at all, exactly as retailer representation was not. Second, the biggest number in the project is the most useless: outlet mall has 295,680 searches a month and the research tool labels it Navigational — shoppers looking for somewhere to go.

24. Rules this guide keeps

  1. One term, one page. Six near-variants ride as secondaries; each was checked on its own results page first, and each returned the same domains as its host.
  2. A service with no search term becomes a section, not a page.
  3. Nothing ships against a term with no live results-page read. The tool's guess at what the searcher wants has been wrong six times here.
  4. Three states, never two. Backed by a search term, not a search target, not researched. A blank cell reads as a zero.
  5. A term missing one input is blocked, however good it looks.
  6. The fourteen teaching pages are not measured on enquiries. They are measured on rankings and links. Judging them on leads will get them killed for doing exactly what they were built to do.

Digital Movement · Husband Retail · 11 August 2026. Tree: 00-sitemap.md. Evidence: ../SEO/husband-keyword-research-2026-08-11.md. Headings, editable: copy-review/seo-sitemap-extended.html.

23.4 The sixth pass — one new page, and the term that answers D7 sideways

Ninety-eight terms measured on 11 August 2026, fifteen live results pages read once Google started serving again. Two cleared.

/landlord-representation is new and is now in the tree. 850 searches a month at competition 1, $4.59 a click, Savills Hong Kong at number one, and it names the exact side the dossier says the firm's paying clients are on. Build condition: nine of thirteen results are "what is landlord representation?" explainers, so the page must answer that in its first two sentences before it sells anything. Treat it like the homepage brief, not like a service page.

retail advisory services cleared and stays a secondary on /retail-consulting — same reader, and two of our own pages should not compete. What it changes is the pillar's opening line: the page that beats everything on that results page is Savills Hong Kong's Retail Landlord Advisory Services, and that phrase describes this client better than "retail consulting" does.

leasing strategy (540 / competition 1) becomes a secondary on /retail-leasing. Half its results page is lease accounting — Binary Stream, a CPA exam question, Thomson Reuters — so the page title has to make the property meaning unmistakable.

retail market report is a publication, not a page. Eight of its nine results are Hong Kong market reports from Knight Frank, Savills, JLL, Cushman & Wakefield, HKTDC and the government statistics office. That is how the competitor set gets found in this market. It belongs in the content plan as a quarterly, and it is a commitment, not a page.

Eleven were blocked, ten of them on the reader. Travel retail is job adverts in Hong Kong. Property asset management is a job title. Retail transformation is software. Lease advisory belongs to a UK government body for residential leaseholders. Retail strategy is coursework. Retail property investment is private investors. Integrated resort is tourists. Restaurant leasing is listings. Luxury retail consulting is brand strategy — healthy, commercial, and work the dossier says the firm does not do. Full verdicts in §12 of the research document.

Do not build a page around a city name. Retail consultant hong kong, property consultant hong kong and retail leasing hong kong have zero searches between them. Hong Kong goes in the writing — the examples, the figures, the buildings — never in the page target.

23.5 The seventh pass — the twenty are in, and one more page

The condition was twenty search terms that are commercially relevant and read on a live results page. That is now met. Nine of the twenty are pages, ten are second phrases inside pages, and one is a quarterly publication. The full list is in §14.2 of the research file and at the foot of §1 of the sitemap.

One new page: /feasibility-studies. Backed by real estate feasibility study — 670 searches a month at competition 8, $4.07 a click. Altus Group, Marsh & Partners and Savills Hong Kong all sell feasibility work on that page, and Savills' own title is Real Estate Feasibility Studies & Development Consultancy — the exact pairing Husband Retail does under the name masterplanning. Write it as the question a landlord actually asks: is this scheme worth building, and at what rent?

Seven pages gain second phrases. Use them in subheadings and body copy, never as a second page:

  • /retail-consulting — retail consultancy, commercial real estate consulting, retail advisory, retail advisory services, property advisory, property advisory services
  • /retail-leasing — leasing services, leasing strategy
  • /insights/commercial-lease-negotiation — lease restructuring
  • /insights/retail-development — mall redevelopment

Two traps this pass exposed.

Say which kind of leasing, in the title. Leasing services puts Colliers, Savills and CBRE Hong Kong in the top three — and then fills the rest of the page with equipment leasing, finance-lease accounting and a Chinese-English dictionary. The same split hits leasing strategy. A title that just says Leasing Services competes with CHG-Meridian. A title that says Retail Property Leasing does not.

Check the industry before the term. Retail brokerage has 1,760 searches a month at $5.69 a click and reads beautifully — and it means stock broking. Singapore Exchange, StashAway and Chief Securities own it. A term can be perfect on every number and belong to somebody else's trade.

And the finding that keeps repeating: the words the industry uses about itself have no demand. Shopping centre consultant, shopping mall consultant, shopping centre strategy, shopping centre operations, retail asset strategy and retail development consultancy are all zero. Put that language in the body copy, where it proves the firm knows its trade. Never build a page to rank for it.

Everything downstream of a confirmed search term: the writing briefs, the page blueprints, the finished copy and the copy prompts. These are page-build documents, not search term research — they are here so the programme has one repository, not because they carry search term evidence.

Page strategy — shopping mall feasibility studies

Absorbed into this document on 12 August 2026 from Website/page-strategy-feasibility.md. Original title: “Page Strategy & SEO Copy Brief — Shopping mall feasibility studies”. That file still exists but this tab is now the master copy.

Phase 4 of the lean process (Phase 6 canonical). This is the writing brief. It is not one of the numbered 01–09 artefacts — it feeds 02 · Page Blueprint.

Client / projectHusband Retail — website redesign
PageShopping mall feasibility studies
Page address/feasibility
PillarDevelopment Consultancy
Ownersearch visibility strategist — Digital Movement UK
Versionv0.1 — 10 August 2026
GateApproval gate: the consultant confirms this page deserves to exist and its strategy is distinct
Next deliverable02 · Page Blueprint

🔴 THIS TERM IS DEAD. The figures were pulled on 10 August 2026 and the term has 10 searches a month worldwide, all of them in South Africa. The gate was not blocked — it has been failed on the evidence. The strategy below is complete and every judgement in it about how to sell this service stays valid; it is the phrase at the top that has to change, not the thinking underneath it. Detail: 01a §0.

Rule for this document: it does not contain final page copy. It decides what the page must do, prove and answer. The words come later, in 03.

1. Business objective

Convert existing authority into enquiries. Husband Retail has links from 142 different websites and roughly 10 unpaid visits a month — the reputation exists, the site does not capture it. This page is the first commercial page of the redesign and its job is to turn a live, winnable search into a first conversation.

Why this page and not another. On the 4 August 2026 verification this is the number one term in the whole set, and the only one where a boutique peer of comparable size has proved the model with a purpose-built page at position 1.

2. Primary audience

An owner, developer or institutional investor with capital not yet committed. A site, a conversion, an extension, or a mall that has stopped performing.

Never a consumer. Never a retailer looking for space. Never a job seeker.

3. Stage of the buyer journey

Evaluation, near the decision. The searcher is not learning what a feasibility study is — the results page is full of consultancies selling one, so the searcher is choosing a supplier. The page's job is comparison, not education.

4. Primary what the searcher is trying to do

Commercial. Verified 4 August 2026 by live results, Hong Kong geo.

Main search term: shopping mall feasibility study consultant

Search volume and competition: verified 10 August 2026, and the answer is no. The exact phrase returns no data at all in the research tool. The nearest measurable phrase, "shopping mall feasibility study", has 10 searches a month worldwide — all of them in South Africa, a market Husband Retail does not serve. The whole "retail feasibility" family totals 80/month across 35 terms; "mall consultant" totals 160/month across 116 terms in the US and 20/month across 51 terms in the UAE.

The earlier note in this file that the research tool was blocked by a stale password was wrong on both counts: the pull ran clean on the first attempt, and the login was never stale — the failures came from using the dead .com.au address instead of office@digitalmovement.uk. Corrected 10 August 2026.

5. What we are walking into

Full analysis: 01a-serp-content-standard-feasibility.md — the competing pages read section by section, 10 August 2026.

The live top 10, 4 August 2026 (SEO/_evidence/husband-serp-verification-2026-08-04.json):

Pos Domain What it is
1rlpc.aeThe page to beat. Boutique Dubai mall consultancy, purpose-built feasibility page
2mfn.comUS feasibility-study writing firm — same phrase, different buyer
3mmcginvest.comUS feasibility writer for SBA/USDA lending — same phrase, different buyer
4scribd.comA student report on Hyderabad — not a competitor
5investinegypt.gov.egAn Egyptian government study summary — not a competitor
6core.ac.ukAn academic paper — not a competitor
7mediaassets.cbre.comCBRE Singapore consulting — a giant, and only at 7
8linkedin.comA LinkedIn page
9wsp.comEngineering and management consultancy
10ollengroup.comBoutique Dubai/KSA mall consultancy

Two things this table hides.

First, three of the top ten are not selling anything — positions 4, 5 and 6 are a student paper, a government PDF and an academic article. The only giant present sits at 7.

Second, and more important: only two of the ten pages target an international mall owner at all. Positions 2 and 3 rank on the same phrase but serve US domestic, lender-driven work — MMCG names SBA 7(a) and 504 loans, DSCR thresholds and bank-required studies outright. A Gulf or Asian developer landing there is in the wrong shop. The real competition is RLPC at 1 and Ollen at 10.

This is a weakly defended results page held together by one peer at the top.

Across the whole field, three things are missing from every page read — see the content standard for the evidence:

  1. Named projects, named clients, evidenced outcomes. Not one page has any.
  2. A serious answer to "what if the study says no." MFN gets closest with one clause and no detail.
  3. Who does the work. In a category selling judgement, nobody names the person exercising it.

All three are things Husband Retail can fill honestly. That is the page.

The page to beat, and what it is missing

RLPC's number one page, read 10 August 2026. Roughly 650 words. Its structure: why feasibility matters · what a study includes · how consultants reduce risk · feasibility is insurance, not a cost · a closing thought · five FAQs.

What it does not have:

  • No named projects. Not one scheme, anywhere on the page.
  • No named clients.
  • No numbers of any kind. No evidence behind a single claim.
  • No real call to action. The promotion is implicit.
  • No answer to the hardest question — what happens when the study says the scheme does not work.
  • No description of the actual deliverable a client receives.

Every one of those gaps is something Husband Retail can fill honestly. That is the whole strategy of this page.

Competitor patterns we must not copy

  • The "insurance, not a cost" argument. RLPC leads on it and it is generic.
  • Writing a definition piece. The searcher already knows what a study is.
  • Listing modelling types as the differentiator — catchment, demographic, sales, risk modelling. Everyone lists them. They are table stakes, not proof.
  • Claiming engineering or masterplanning capability. WSP owns that ground and Husband Retail does not do it.
  • Any superlative. Half this results page is superlatives with nothing behind them, which is exactly the credibility gap we are exploiting.

6. Primary topic

Whether a specific shopping mall scheme will work — answered by people who have not only planned destinations but leased them.

7. Supporting terminology

Use naturally, never forced: retail feasibility study · catchment · tenant demand · tenant mix · scheme viability · gross lettable area · anchor and flagship tenants · positioning · phasing · leasing strategy · repositioning · trade area · footfall.

Avoid as page framing: "solutions", "end-to-end offering", "value proposition", "data-driven insights".

8. The questions this page must answer

Drawn from the live results page and from what an owner actually asks. Five to seven become the FAQ; the rest are answered in the body.

  1. What does a shopping mall feasibility study actually tell me?
  2. What do I receive at the end — what is the deliverable?
  3. How long does one take? (evidence-dependent — see §16)
  4. What is it based on? Where do the numbers come from?
  5. Will you tell me if the scheme does not work?
  6. Who does the work — the founder, or a team I have not met?
  7. Do you have a commercial interest in the answer?
  8. At what stage should a study be commissioned?
  9. How is this different from what CBRE or JLL would produce?
  10. Which markets do you cover?

9. Objections, in the order the buyer feels them

# Objection Where the page handles it
1"The study will just confirm what I hoped, because that is what I paid for."The "when the answer is no" section
2"It will be desk research, not grounded in who will actually take the space."How we reach the answer — the leasing evidence
3"The adviser is really a broker and the report is the sales call."The leasing discipline: only leasing for developments worked on
4"It will stop at the drawing and never name a tenant."Proof — named schemes, tenants placed
5"I will meet the founder and be handed juniors."How we reach the answer — small and senior
6"A boutique cannot match a CBRE report."Why owners choose a boutique

Never name an objection out loud. Answer it.

10. Required proof

Permitted — dossier §10:

  • Founded 1998, Hong Kong, boutique retail real-estate consultancy.
  • 100+ projects advised; 60+ planned and leased, across Asia, the Middle East and Europe.
  • Named destinations: Landmark HK, Elements HK, Galaxy Macau, DLF Emporio, MahaNakhon, Covent Garden, Seoul IFC, Lusail.
  • Named clients: Hongkong Land, MTR, DLF, Capco, AIG Global RE, PNB, Value Retail, Galaxy + KKR, Brookfield, Morgan Stanley, Taubman, Westfield.
  • Paul Husband: The Cult of the Luxury Brand (2006), ICSC instructor, KODW 2019, 30+ years including Swire / Pacific Place.
  • The leasing discipline: "we only handle leasing for developments we have worked on."

Forbidden — dossier §11. Do not put these on the page:

  • Footfall figures, floor areas, the Covent Garden "4×/5×", opening dates — all single-sourced to Husband Retail's own old site.
  • Any "world's largest" or comparable superlative.
  • Kura Kura Bali. The Asia Retail Centre Council, ARRC congress and Global Retail Report — these are Digital Movement proposals, not Husband Retail facts.
  • Revenue, headcount, fee structure, current active mandates.
  • Anything present-tense about live mandates until trading status is confirmed.

The strongest proof available is the leasing record, because it is the one thing on this results page nobody else can claim. A study from a firm that has signed the tenants is a different object from a study by a firm that has not.

11. Conversion

PrimaryEnquiry form — a first conversation about a specific scheme
SecondaryRead a named case study; read the founder's page
Form placementHero right column and page foot. Nowhere else.
FieldsName · phone (required) · email (optional) · scheme or location · message · consent checkbox, unticked
Friction to removeDo not ask for budget, timeline or company size on first contact

12. Recommended section sequence, and the purpose of each

Adapted from the framework's standard service-page model. Departures are noted.

# Section Purpose Status
1HeroConfirm they are in the right place. H1 carries the search phrase naturally. Form to the right.Mandatory
2The question a study has to settleName the real decision in plain words. Not a definition.Mandatory
3What the study covers, and what you receiveThe deliverable, named and concrete. The #1 page has nothing here.Mandatory
4How we reach the answerSequence, first call to handover. Who does the work. Where the judgement comes from.Mandatory
5When the answer is noShort. The willingness to say a scheme does not work. Nobody on this results page says it.Mandatory — the differentiator
6ProofTwo or three named schemes: the brief, what Husband Retail did, what changed.Mandatory
7Why owners choose a boutiqueHonest comparison against the giants and the brokerages.Mandatory
8Questions people ask5–7 from §8, 40–60 words each. FAQPage schema.Mandatory
9Related services and readingInternal links out.Optional
10CloseSecond form, plain invitation.Mandatory

Departure from the standard model: "outcomes" and "service scope" are merged into one section (3), and a section the standard does not have — "when the answer is no" (5) — is added. It is added because it is the single strongest trust signal available and no competing page on this results page carries it.

Sections deliberately left out, and why

  • Pricing or fee ranges — house rule, and dossier §11 forbids fee structure.
  • A promised turnaround time — we have no evidenced figure. Goes back in only if the client confirms one in writing.
  • Testimonials — none are attributed or permissioned.
  • A downloadable sample report — none exists.
  • Team biographies beyond Paul Husband — the institutional-finance credential in dossier §10 belongs to someone who has since left the firm.
  • A methodology name or proprietary framework — inventing one would be a fabricated claim.

13. Required link between our own pages

In: /development-consultancy · /leasing · the projects hub · homepage services block.

Out: /development-consultancy (parent pillar) · /leasing · two or three named project case studies · /paul · /contact.

16. Copy-length guidance

Roughly 850–1,100 words as a sanity check, not a target. Version 1 is written from the blueprint before layout exists; exact character counts are fitted at v2, once components are real. Never write v1 to an imaginary budget.

For context: the page currently at number one is about 650 words with no evidence in it.

2. The content standard

Read 10 August 2026. Three pages read in full, one blocked.

Element RLPC #1 MFN #2 MMCG #3 Ollen #10 Standard
Why feasibility matters✅✅✅not checkedTable stakes
What a study includes✅✅✅not checkedTable stakes
Describes the deliverable❌✅ full⚠️ partialnot checkedCommon
A named process or stages❌✅❌not checkedDifferentiator
FAQ✅ 5 Qs❌❌not checkedDifferentiator
Price shown❌❌✅ from $4,900not checkedRare
Turnaround time shown❌❌✅ 9–16 daysnot checkedRare
Explicit call to action❌ implicit✅ ×2✅not checkedCommon
Named projects❌❌❌not checkedAbsent everywhere
Named clients❌❌❌not checkedAbsent everywhere
Evidenced outcomes❌❌ hypothetical only❌not checkedAbsent everywhere
What happens when the answer is no❌⚠️ one clause❌not checkedAbsent everywhere
Word count~650~2,200~1,100not checked650–2,200

Ollen Group returned HTTP 403 on two attempts. Every Ollen cell above reads not checked, never "absent". We have not looked; that is a different claim from having looked and found nothing.

3. What this tells us

The floor. Any page here must explain why feasibility matters before capital is committed, and what a study includes. Leave either out and the page reads as thin against the field.

Length is not the lever. The number one page is the shortest of the three by a wide margin — 650 words against MFN's 2,200. Writing long to win here would be copying the wrong signal. Our 850–1,100 word guidance stands.

The opening is evidence. Not one of the three pages names a single project, a single client, or a single real outcome. MFN's only figure is a hypothetical $10,000 budget for a hotel. This is a category selling judgement with nothing behind it.

Husband Retail can name Landmark, Elements, Galaxy Macau, Seoul IFC, DLF Emporio, MahaNakhon, Covent Garden and Lusail, and clients including Hongkong Land, MTR, DLF, Capco, Brookfield, Morgan Stanley, Taubman and Westfield. On the one axis where the entire field is empty, we are the only ones holding anything.

The second opening is the negative answer. No page seriously addresses what happens when the study concludes the scheme does not work. MFN gets closest with a single clause — "if a negative picture is portrayed, then you would stop development" — and says nothing about how that conclusion is reached or delivered. For a buyer whose first fear is a bought verdict, that silence is the gap.

The third opening is who does the work. Nobody says. In a field where the product is judgement, no page names the person exercising it.

4. Patterns not to copy

  • "Feasibility is insurance, not a cost." RLPC leads on it. Generic.
  • Definition writing. The searcher already knows what a study is.
  • Listing modelling types as the differentiator — catchment, demographic, sales, risk. Every page lists them. Table stakes, not proof.
  • US lender vocabulary — SBA, DSCR, Monte Carlo simulation. Right for MMCG's buyer, wrong for ours, and using it would pull the wrong enquiries.
  • Publishing a price or a turnaround time — MMCG does both. House rule forbids pricing, and we have no evidenced duration.
  • Engineering and masterplanning claims. WSP's ground, not ours.

5. Confidence and gaps

Claim Basis Confidence
Only two of ten results target our buyerResult titles plus three pages read in fullHigh
Named projects and clients absent across the fieldThree of four competitor pages readHigh for those three; unknown for Ollen
Nobody addresses the negative answerSame three pagesSame caveat
Positions 4, 5, 6, 8 are non-commercialResult titles and domains only — pages not openedMedium
Search volume and competitionNot verified — the research tool blocked on five routesNone. No figure may be estimated.

To close the Ollen gap: it needs a route that is not WebFetch — Firecrawl scrape with the key in the outreach .env, or a logged-in browser. Until then the finding stands on three pages, and the document says so.

What the searcher wants: mixed, leaning commercial, and weakly defended by specialists. The buying intent is genuine — five results sell services and three are global giants. But only two results address the person who owns the property. The rest of the page is generalists selling operations consulting to retailers, plus career and definition content that converts nobody.

Does that match the page we intend to build? Yes — and the mismatch that does exist is the angle. The page must resolve the buyer question in its first two lines: this is for the people who own the property, not the people who sell the shoes. A visitor who wanted supply-chain consulting should know within one sentence that they are in the wrong place, and a mall owner should know within the same sentence that they are in exactly the right one.

What the tool guesses the searcher wants: the research tool says "Informational + Commercial." Recorded, not accepted as the verdict — the live page shows real commercial competition. Note also that the research tool labels the near-identical "retail consultant" as purely Informational while labelling four of its own variations Commercial. That internal disagreement is exactly why the results page decides.

0c · The finding that decided the term

Husband Retail already ranks #7 for this term in Hong Kong — with its homepage, not a service page; on HTTP, not HTTPS; on a site whose footer reads © 1998–2020 and whose last modification date is 20 October 2020; with a the research tool's site-strength rating of 5–6 and roughly ten unpaid visits a month.

That is the cheapest ranking opportunity in the entire project. Google already associates this firm with this term on the worst possible evidence. A purpose-built, current, secure page with real proof on it is not a speculative bet — it is upgrading an asset that is already half-working.

1. Which results are actually competitors

Only the pages selling this service to our buyer count. Three of the five commercial results sell to a different buyer entirely.

Domain Category Competitor? Why
knightfrank.com.hkProperty giant, retail consultancy for HKYes — the direct peerSame buyer, same service, same city
bain.comStrategy giantSame phrase, different buyerSells transformation to retail C-suite
mckinsey.comStrategy giantSame phrase, different buyerSame
accenture.comTechnology and strategy giantSame phrase, different buyerSells AI and digital transformation to retailers
consultancy.asia, g-co.agencyDirectoriesNoWorth a listing, not a fight
ascentpros, umbrex ×2, waterhousewadeCareers, definitions, recruiter blogNoWrong reader entirely

Fewer than half the results sell to our buyer, and only one of them is a real peer. On the framework's own test, this results page is weakly defended for the specific position Husband Retail occupies. The giants are unbeatable on domain strength and irrelevant on subject matter; the one true peer is beatable on content, as §2 shows.

2. The content standard

Pages read in full: Knight Frank HK, Bain, Accenture. Read 10 August 2026.

Element Knight Frank Bain Accenture Standard
Services list✅✅✅Table stakes
A "why us" block✅✅✅Table stakes
Named contact person✅ Helen Mak✅ 8 partners with photos✅ 2 leadersTable stakes
Contact CTA✅ form, repeated✅ "Ready to talk?"✅ "Learn more" linksTable stakes
Insights / publications block✅✅✅Table stakes
Sector or segment breakdown❌✅ grocery, fashion, restaurants✅Common
Real numbers, evidenced⚠️ one GDP stat from 2021✅ 3,900 projects · 70% of top-50 retailers · 20–40% cost cuts✅ four consumer statisticsCommon
Named clients❌✅ Target✅ Noli, H&M FoundationCommon
Describes what you actually receive❌✅ a seven-pillar diagnostic⚠️ outcomes, not methodDifferentiator
Named property projects — a centre they planned and leased❌❌❌ABSENT EVERYWHERE
What happens when the answer is no❌❌❌ABSENT EVERYWHERE
FAQ❌❌❌ABSENT EVERYWHERE
Price shown❌❌❌Absent everywhere
Turnaround shown❌❌❌Absent everywhere
Word count~1,200~3,200~2,100–2,500—

3. What this tells us

The floor. A services list, a reason to choose us, a named human being, a way to make contact, and something to read next. Leave any of those out and the page reads as thinner than the field.

Is length a lever? No. The two longest pages are Bain and Accenture, and they rank on domain authority, not word count. Knight Frank ranks at 11 on ~1,200 words. Writing 3,000 words to match Bain would be copying a signal we cannot benefit from. Target 1,100–1,400 words, all of it load-bearing.

The openings — three, and all three are honest for this client.

  1. Named projects with named clients. Not one page on this results page names a shopping centre it planned. Husband Retail can name The Landmark (Hongkong Land), Elements (MTR), Galaxy Macau (Galaxy Entertainment), DLF Emporio (DLF — India's first international-standard luxury centre), Seoul IFC (AIG Global Real Estate), MahaNakhon (Pace), Covent Garden (Capco), Lusail Boulevard (Qatari Diar). Dossier §4 and §10 permit all of these.
  2. A straight answer to who this is for. Every page here is vague about the buyer, because the giants want both retailers and landlords. Husband Retail only serves one of them, and saying so is a filter, not a limitation.
  3. A published question-and-answer section. Nobody on this results page has one.

Evidence density. The giants back their claims and the property-side peer does not: Knight Frank's single number is a GDP forecast from 2021, five years stale on a page live in 2026. Husband Retail's advantage here is real but must be handled carefully — see the constraint below.

The constraint that shapes everything. Dossier §11: the firm's own site is frozen at © 2020, and the project outcome figures (Covent Garden's "4× spend, 5× capital value", floor areas, opening dates) are single-sourced to that site. Until the client second-sources them, the proof section runs on named projects and named clients — which are verifiable and impressive on their own — and not on outcome percentages. Names are permitted. Numbers claiming results are not.

4. Patterns not to copy

  • The giants' abstraction. "Reinvent retail", "envision the future", "partners in change". It is what a firm writes when it cannot name what it did.
  • Consultant vocabulary aimed at the wrong buyer — omnichannel, supply chain, transformation, zero-based budgeting. Every one of those words tells a mall owner this page is not for them.
  • A stale statistic used as proof. Knight Frank's 2021 GDP forecast is worse than no number at all.
  • Naming every service as a bulleted list and calling it a capability. Four practice areas exist; the page has to show what they do together, not list them.
  • Superlatives from the old site — "world's largest", "iconic" used as filler. Dossier §11 flags these as single-sourced.
  • Fee ranges or turnaround promises. House rule, and nobody in the field publishes them either.

5. Confidence and gaps

Claim Basis Confidence
Volume, country split, competitionthe research tool, pulled 10 Aug 2026High
Intent verdict and results page compositionLive Google as a searcher in Hong Kong sees it, 11 results read 10 Aug 2026High
Husband Retail ranks #7Same live pull; URL confirmed as husband-retail.com homepageHigh
Knight Frank / Bain / Accenture page contentsAll three read in full 10 Aug 2026High
Husband Retail's own current page contentNot checked this session — the live site is HTTP-only and refuses the HTTPS upgrade our fetcher forcesNone — recorded as not checked, not as absent
Project names, clients, scopeknowledge-base/husband-retail-dossier.md §4, §5, §10High for names; single-source for outcome figures (§11)

Pages not read, and why. husband-retail.com — HTTP-only, no HTTPS redirect; our fetcher upgrades and the host does not answer. Closing this needs a plain-HTTP crawl or the logged-in browser. It does not block this document: the dossier already carries the site's content, compiled 23 June 2026.

Definition of Done

  • 🔴 Global search volume recorded, with country breakdown, source and date — 4,000/mo worldwide
  • 🔴 Volume in the client's own markets recorded separately — HK 70/mo across 47 terms
  • 🔴 Competition score recorded, with source and date — 36%, "Possible"
  • 🔴 What the searcher wants read off the live results page, verdict written, match confirmed — mixed, commercial-leaning; matches with the buyer stated up front
  • Every result on page one classified
  • Every direct competitor page read in full, or marked not checked with a reason
  • Matrix complete, no blank cell where a page was read
  • Each element classified
  • Word counts recorded for every page read
  • At least one honest opening identified — three
  • Patterns-not-to-copy list written
  • Unverified figures labelled, no estimate substituted
  • Findings carried into 02 §3 and §7

Section 3 is the important part: real content, top to bottom, in priority order, with no layout at all. Its value is that it cannot look finished, so it cannot be signed off prematurely the way a wireframe can.

1. Page purpose

This page exists to make the visitor do one thing:

Start a conversation about a specific scheme — a site, a conversion, an extension, or a mall that has stopped performing.

Primary conversion action

Complete the enquiry form naming the scheme or its location.

Secondary action, for people not ready yet

Read a named case study, or read the founder's page. Both build the case that the judgement being sold has been exercised before, on schemes they recognise.

How this page relates to the rest of the site

Sections we deliberately left out, and why

  • Pricing or fee ranges — house rule; dossier §11 forbids publishing fee structure. MMCG shows "from $4,900"; we do not follow.
  • A promised turnaround time — MMCG publishes 9–16 business days. We have no evidenced figure. Goes in only if the client confirms one in writing.
  • Testimonials — none are attributed or permissioned.
  • A downloadable sample report — none exists.
  • Team biographies beyond Paul Husband — the institutional-finance credential in dossier §10 belongs to someone who has since left the firm.
  • A named proprietary methodology — inventing one would be a fabricated claim.
  • A definition-led opener — the field's default. The searcher already knows what a feasibility study is.
  • Modelling-type lists as the differentiator — catchment, demographic, sales, risk. Every competitor lists them. Table stakes, not proof.

This is the pillar page for the whole consultancy offer. The four practice areas — development consultancy, leasing, positioning, investment and asset management — become child pages beneath it. Everything about how this page is built becomes the pattern the others inherit.

2. Why this term, in one paragraph

The previous target, "shopping mall feasibility study consultant", was chosen on what the results page looked like and killed on 10 August 2026 by the demand figures: 10 searches a month worldwide, all of them in South Africa. "Retail consulting" is the opposite case — 4,000 searches a month worldwide, spread across markets the firm actually works in, competition 36 ("possible"), and a results page where only two of eleven entries speak to a property owner. Husband Retail is already one of those two, at position 7, with a homepage on an insecure server last touched in 2020. Full evidence in 01a §0.

What changed in v2, and why. v1 was written to the standing house rule that all copy must be understood by a fifteen-year-old. The client overrode that rule for this page on 10 August 2026. See §0 — the audience evidence made the case.

0. Register — who actually reads this page

The house plain-language rule exists for consumer and patient audiences. This page has neither. The register below is set from the client's own ABM list, not from preference.

Evidence: ABM/husband-target-accounts.csv (1,170 accounts) and ABM/husband-target-stakeholders.csv (3,282 named stakeholders).

SeniorityC-suite 47% · director 31% · head of function 18% · manager 4%
Rolesexecutive 54% · development 17% · leasing 8% · retail 7% · asset management 6% · strategy 5% · investment 3%
Organisation typelandlord/owner 42% · developer 24% · REIT 15% · operator, master developer, integrated resort, department store group, transport retail, sovereign
RegionEurope 22% · SE Asia 15% · Gulf 11% · Greater China 10% · South Asia 9% · North America 9% · North Asia 8% · Africa 6% · Oceania 5% · Latin America 5%
Top HQ countriesIndia, United States, Japan, China, United Kingdom, Germany, UAE, Australia, Malaysia, Saudi Arabia
Commonest titlesManaging Director · CEO · Executive Director · CFO · Development Director · Chief Investment Officer · Director of Leasing · Head of Asset Management · Board Member · Owner

The two facts that set the register, and they pull in opposite directions:

  1. The reader is a principal, not a practitioner. 96% are head of function or above. Explaining "trade area" to a Development Director is not clarity, it is condescension, and it signals that the writer has never done the work.
  2. Most read English as a second language. The list is majority non-Anglophone: India, Japan, China, Germany, UAE, Turkey, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia.

So: raise the vocabulary, not the grammar. Industry nouns are used and never glossed. Sentences stay short, one idea each, few subordinate clauses. The complexity lives entirely in the terminology.

Assumed known, never explained: trade area · catchment · spend capture · tenant mix · retail mix · merchandising plan · zoning · adjacencies · GLA · anchor · pre-leasing · heads of terms · lease documentation · repositioning · asset plan · mixed-use · master plan · capex · underwriting · feasibility · mandate · principal · occupier · instruct · appointment.

Deliberately left plain. Four lines resisted the lift and were kept flat, because bluntness is the effect: "A plan nobody signs is just paper." · "None of this is a claim about ourselves. It is a list you can check." · "Offices in every market, data on everything." · the pull quote "We will not lease a centre we did not help plan."

1. Hero

H1

Retail consulting for the people who own the asset

Subheadline

Husband Retail plans, leases and repositions luxury retail destinations for developers, landlords and investors. More than 100 projects in over 20 markets since 1998.

Trust line

The Landmark for Hongkong Land. Elements for MTR. Galaxy Macau for Galaxy Entertainment. Planned and leased by this firm.

2. Who we act for

H2 Who we act for

We act for the principals in retail property: developers, landlords, asset managers and institutional investors. Our clients develop shopping centres, lifestyle destinations, integrated resorts and mixed-use schemes, or they acquire and manage them.

We do not advise retailers on the occupier side: store networks, formats, operations. The strategy houses do that work, and this is not the page for it. If you hold the asset, or intend to, it is.

3. What we do

H2 Four practices, one piece of work

A retail destination is one continuous piece of work, and we treat it that way.

H3 Development consultancy

It starts with the market: trade area, catchment, competing supply and spend capture. From that study come the concept, the positioning, the tenant mix and a merchandising plan that zones the GLA so the whole asset trades.

H3 Leasing

Then we lease it. Leasing strategy, project marketing, negotiation and documentation, from heads of terms to signature. A plan nobody signs is just paper.

H3 Positioning and repositioning

Once the centre opens, the work changes but does not stop. We set the positioning, run the marketing programme and write the asset plan that holds the tenant mix to the concept.

H3 Investment advisory

For investors, we advise on acquisitions, disposals and the business planning behind them: feasibility, the retail assumptions in the underwriting, the asset plan the deal depends on.

Closing line

Four practice areas. One job.

4. The places we have done it

H2 The record

Most consultancies describe their method. We would rather name the buildings.

  • The Landmark, Hong Kong. Consultancy, leasing and brand marketing for Hongkong Land at its flagship luxury centre in Central.
  • Elements, Hong Kong. Consultancy and leasing for MTR Corporation's mixed-use scheme above Kowloon station.
  • Galaxy Macau. Consultancy and leasing on the Phase 2 retail for Galaxy Entertainment.
  • DLF Emporio, New Delhi. Consultancy and leasing for DLF Universal on India's first international-standard luxury centre.
  • Suzhou Village and Shanghai Village. Consultancy and leasing for Value Retail, including the village within the Shanghai Disney resort.
  • Seoul IFC Mall. Consultancy for AIG Global Real Estate on Korea's first world-standard luxury mall.
  • Covent Garden, London. Consultancy and leasing for Capco on the repositioning of the estate.
  • Lusail Boulevard, Doha. Consultancy and leasing for Qatari Diar.
  • Merdeka 118, Kuala Lumpur. Consultancy and leasing for PNB.

We have also acted for New World Development, Hysan, Link REIT, Ayala Land, Central Retail, Taubman, Westfield, KKR, Brookfield, Morgan Stanley and China Resources.

Into the centres we planned we have brought Louis Vuitton, Chanel, Dior, Hermès, Cartier, Prada, Tiffany and Co. and Burberry, among others.

None of this is a claim about ourselves. It is a list you can check.

5. How we work

H2 How an engagement runs

It begins with a conversation about one asset. Yours. We ask what you hold or intend to build, where it stands in its cycle, and what the mandate should deliver. If we can do the work, we scope it and agree it with you before anything starts.

The people you meet in that first conversation do the work. Husband Retail is a small firm by design. Paul Husband leads every mandate, and the team around him has both planned centres and leased them. That combination is rarer than it sounds. Someone who has negotiated heads of terms with a luxury brand's head of real estate writes a very different merchandising plan from someone who has only modelled one.

The work then runs in sequence: feasibility, concept, merchandising plan, leasing, opening, asset plan. One team from first meeting to handover, the same senior people throughout.

The six steps

# Step Gloss
01FeasibilityTrade area, catchment, competing supply
02ConceptPositioning, retail mix, anchors
03Merchandising planZoning, adjacencies, unit strategy
04LeasingHeads of terms to signature
05OpeningLaunch marketing, trading readiness
06Asset planThe plan the centre trades to

6. Why a boutique and not a giant

H2 Why a boutique and not a giant

The large property firms are good at what they do. Offices in every market, data on everything. For valuations, agency work at scale and research, they are often the right appointment.

We offer something narrower. Senior attention on one asset at a time, and one discipline that shapes everything: we only handle leasing for developments we have worked on.

Pull quote

We will not lease a centre we did not help plan.

So we cannot hand you a plan we would not have to deliver ourselves. Every recommendation in our reports is one we may have to sell across the table later. That keeps the advice honest.

It also keeps us small. We take few mandates and give each the founder's time. If you want your asset treated as one file among hundreds, we are the wrong appointment.

7. Questions principals ask

H2 Questions principals ask

H3 Do you act for owners or for occupiers?

For owners only. Developers, landlords and investors instruct us. We negotiate with brands on behalf of the asset, never the reverse. The advice carries no occupier conflict.

H3 What happens if the feasibility work says the scheme does not stack up?

We tell you, in writing, before you commit capex. It has happened. A study that only ever says yes is worthless. When the answer is no, we set out what would have to change for it to become yes.

H3 We have already mandated a global agency. Where do you fit?

Often alongside them. We have worked with the international firms many times, usually holding the concept, the retail mix and the luxury leasing while they run the rest of the scheme. We are clear about the division of scope.

H3 Who runs the engagement day to day?

The senior people you meet at the start. Paul Husband leads every mandate personally. We do not run a model where partners win the work and pass it down. The firm stays small so that this stays true.

H3 Can you bring Louis Vuitton or Hermès into our asset?

We have recruited both, along with Chanel, Cartier, Prada and others, into centres we planned. No adviser can commit a named brand. We can shape the asset those brands ask for, then make the case to people we have dealt with for years.

H3 Our centre is trading below its catchment. Is repositioning still viable?

Usually, yes. Repositioning is one of our four practice areas. It starts as a new project does, with an honest read of the trade area and the existing mix, and ends with a plan for the leasing and the marketing programme.

H3 Which markets do you cover?

We have advised in more than 20 markets across Asia, the Middle East and Europe, from Hong Kong and Seoul to Doha, Dubai and London. We are based in Hong Kong and travel to the asset.

8. Paul Husband

Eyebrow The founder

Lead line

More than 30 years in Asian retail real estate, first at Swire Properties, then under his own name.

Paul Husband was head of marketing at Swire Properties for around ten years, joining in 1988 with Pacific Place in Hong Kong. He founded Husband Retail in 1998 and has since planned more than 100 retail projects across Asia Pacific and the Middle East. He wrote The Cult of the Luxury Brand in 2006 and taught as a professional instructor for the International Council of Shopping Centres in Asia Pacific.

Link label About Paul Husband

Copy prompt — feasibility (trial)

Absorbed into this document on 12 August 2026 from SEO/service-page-01-feasibility-COPY-PROMPT.md. Original title: “Husband Retail — Service page 01, copy prompt (trial)”. That file still exists but this tab is now the master copy.

Date: 10 August 2026 Page: Shopping mall feasibility studies · page address /feasibility Primary search phrase: shopping mall feasibility study consultant Pillar: Development Consultancy Status: trial copy. Four inputs open — see §9. None of them block the draft.

Why this term first

It is the top term in the 4 August 2026 re-tiered list, and the only one in the whole set where a boutique peer of comparable size has proved the model:

  • RLPC holds position 1 with a purpose-built feasibility page.
  • mmcginvest and MFN also sell the service on that results page.
  • CBRE only reaches position 7 — a giant is beatable here.

So the page is not an experiment in whether people search this. Someone Husband Retail's size already wins it with exactly the page we are about to write.

Two things this page is not: it is not the Development Consultancy pillar page (that comes next, at /development-consultancy), and it is not the kw01 investment-advisory page already in production, which is a separate wedge play on the firm's weakest pillar.

1. What a copy prompt for this page needs

Nine inputs. Eight are in the project already; the ninth is a short list of things only Paul can answer, and the draft is written so it does not wait for him.

# Input Where it lives Ready?
1Page, one search phrase, page addressThis document✅
2The angle — what the results page shows, and where we fit§3✅
3Who reads it, and what they arrive worried about§4✅
4Voice sample and tone rulesHomepage prototype index.html + dossier §7✅
5Fact boundary — what we may claim, what we may notDossier §10 and §11✅
6Heading plan, word budget, calls to action§6✅
7House rules (plain language, low load, form rules)§7✅
8Output shape and file names§8✅
9Real numbers and current trading statusOpen — §9⚠️

2. The rule that governs everything

The writer must draft the whole page from research alone, without needing Paul Husband. His time is the bottleneck. Every genuine client-input item goes in one short list at the foot of the draft — never scattered through it as blanks.

3. The angle

The people ranking here are consultancies selling the same service. The reader is not being educated; they are choosing who to hire. So the page's job is comparison, not persuasion.

What Husband Retail can honestly say that RLPC and CBRE cannot:

  • It has leased what it planned. 60+ destinations planned and leased, 100+ advised. Most feasibility work stops at the model. Husband Retail's numbers come from having signed the tenants.
  • The luxury tenant relationships are first-hand, not a forecast. When the study says a scheme can hold that calibre of brand, it is a judgement made by someone who has placed them.
  • It is small and senior. The person who advises is the person who does the work. No handover to juniors after the pitch.
  • It refuses work. "We only handle leasing for developments we have worked on." A study from Husband Retail is not a sales pitch for a leasing mandate on a scheme they had no part in shaping.

Do not claim engineering, cost consultancy, structural or masterplanning capability. Do not claim to be cheaper or faster. Do not describe a proprietary model or methodology name that does not exist.

4. Who reads it

An owner, developer or investor with money not yet committed. A site, a conversion, an extension, or a mall that has stopped working. The question underneath the search is always some version of: can this actually work, and who will tell me the truth about it?

Their four worries, in the order they feel them:

  1. That the study will simply confirm what they hoped, because that is what they paid for.
  2. That the numbers will be desk research, not grounded in who will actually take the space.
  3. That the adviser is really a broker, and the report is the sales call.
  4. That it stops at the drawing and never names a tenant.

Answer all four in the copy. Never name them as objections.

5. Voice

Take the tone straight from the homepage prototype at Web Design Projects/Husband Retail Homepage/index.html.

Premium, understated, confident, restrained. British spelling. Formal but warm. Quietly authoritative. Never salesy. Short sentences. Concrete beats abstract: "we lifted footfall at X by Y%" beats "we improve performance".

Banned: growth-agency language, exclamation marks, "unlock", "elevate", "seamless", "cutting-edge", "world-class", "leverage", "bespoke solutions", stacked adjectives, rhetorical questions used as headings, and any superlative we cannot evidence.

6. Structure — the priority guide

This is the priority guide that belongs in the 02 · Page Blueprint (see §8). Roughly 850–1,100 words in total, but treat that as a sanity check, not a target: the standard says v1 is written from the blueprint, before layout, and exact character counts are only fitted at v2 once the real components exist. Never write to an imaginary character budget.

One idea per section. Every heading tells a skimmer what the section is about — no teasers, no wordplay.

  1. Hero — H1 carrying the search phrase naturally, one sentence saying what we do and for whom, one call to action. Copy in the left column, enquiry form in the right (house standard).
  2. What a feasibility study answers — 80–120 words. The actual questions a study settles, in plain words.
  3. What you receive — the deliverable, named. Sections of the report, roughly how long it takes, what it is based on. Concrete.
  4. How we reach the answer — the sequence, first call to handover. Say who does the work. This is where "we have leased what we planned" earns its place.
  5. When the answer is no — short. The willingness to say a scheme does not work is the strongest trust signal on the page and nobody else on that results page says it.
  6. Proof — two or three named schemes from the approved list. What the brief was, what Husband Retail did, what changed. Figures only if evidenced.
  7. Questions people ask — 5–7, drawn from the real results page and People Also Ask, answered in 40–60 words each.
  8. Close — the second enquiry form, plain invitation.

Internal links: /development-consultancy, /leasing, the relevant project case studies, /paul.

7. House rules that apply

  • Plain language, no jargon — a fifteen-year-old must follow it.
  • Low cognitive load standards (skills low-cognitive-load-copy, low-cognitive-load-design).
  • Human-sounding copy: skill web-copy-craft — structural tells, not word lists.
  • Hero fills the first two thirds of the screen, never the whole screen.
  • Two enquiry forms only: hero right column and page foot. Phone required, email optional, consent field present.
  • No pricing anywhere. No invented figures. Nothing from dossier §11.
  • Ship gate before it is called done: skill seo-page-qa-gate.
  • Written with Fable 5 at very high thinking effort — announce the switch.

The house standard lives at Operations/Web Project Operating Process/. Templates are in templates/, per-project copies in _project-starter/. None of them have been started for Husband Retail yet. Copy is not a loose markdown file in this process — it is deliverable 03, with a fixed shape.

9. The four open inputs

  1. Closed 10 August 2026 — and it closed badly. Volume and competition were pulled cleanly from the research tool in the logged-in browser. The exact phrase returns no data at all; the nearest measurable phrase, "shopping mall feasibility study", has 10 searches a month worldwide, all of them in South Africa. This prompt must not be run against this term. The craft guidance in it — angle, reader, voice, house rules — carries over intact to whichever term replaces it. The earlier claim that the research tool password was stale was wrong: the failures came from the dead .com.au login address.
  2. Is the firm still actively trading in 2026? The old site is frozen at © 2020. Confirm before writing anything present-tense about live mandates.
  3. Project figures are single-sourced. Footfall, floor areas, the Covent Garden "4×/5×" and the opening dates all trace to Husband Retail's own old site. Either evidence them elsewhere or write the proof section without figures.
  4. One short list at the foot of the draft — the few things only Paul can answer, gathered in one place.

Copy prompt — development consultancy

Absorbed into this document on 12 August 2026 from SEO/service-page-01-development-consultancy-COPY-PROMPT.md. Original title: “Husband Retail — Service page 01, copy prompt”. That file still exists but this tab is now the master copy.

Date: 10 August 2026 Page: Development Consultancy (pillar 1 of 4) · page address /development-consultancy Primary search phrase: shopping mall concept development consultant Status: prompt ready to run. Four inputs still open — see §9.

Why this page first

The redesign needs the four pillar pages whatever else happens, and Development Consultancy is the pillar Husband Retail is strongest and oldest on (1998, the Pacific Place / Landmark lineage). Its search phrase also sits in the build-first tier of the 4 August verification: WSP #1, RLPC #2, Kinetic, The Mall Company, Ollen #8 — a real advisory results page at a comparable scale, not a giant-only one.

"Shopping mall feasibility study consultant" is the strongest single term we found (RLPC holds #1 with a purpose-built page), but it deserves its own page, /feasibility, built second. Putting both phrases on one page waters down each. This page mentions feasibility and links to it.

The kw01 page already in production ("shopping mall investment advisory Asia Pacific") is a separate track — a wedge play on the weakest pillar. It is not the first pillar page of the redesign.

1. What the prompt must contain (the checklist)

A copy prompt for this page is only as good as these nine inputs. Seven exist today; four items inside them are open (§9).

# Input Where it lives Ready?
1Page, single search phrase, page addressThis document✅
2The angle — what the search results say and why we are not fighting head-onThis document §3✅
3Who reads it and what they arrive worried about§4✅
4Voice sample and tone rulesHomepage prototype index.html + dossier §7✅
5The fact boundary — what we may claim, what we may notDossier §10 and §11✅
6Structure: heading plan, word budget, calls to action§6✅
7House rules (plain language, low load, form rules)Skills, listed in §7✅
8Output shape and where the file goes§8✅
9Real project numbers and current trading statusOpen — §9⚠️

2. The one rule that governs everything

The writer must be able to draft the whole page from research alone, without needing Paul Husband. His time is the bottleneck. Every genuine client-input item goes into one short list at the foot of the draft, never scattered through it as blanks.

3. The angle

Google's results for this phrase are held by engineering and planning consultancies (WSP) and by boutique mall advisers (RLPC, Ollen, Kinetic, The Mall Company). So the reader is already shopping for a consultant. They are not being educated; they are choosing.

That makes the page's job comparison, not persuasion. The differences Husband Retail can honestly claim over that field:

  • It has actually planned and leased the destinations it talks about — 60+ planned and leased, 100+ advised. Most of the field consults; few have signed the tenants.
  • The luxury tenant relationships are real and first-hand, not a promise.
  • It is small and senior. The person who advises is the person who does it.
  • The leasing discipline: "we only handle leasing for developments we have worked on." That is a refusal, which reads as trustworthy.

Do not claim to be bigger, faster or cheaper. Do not claim technical engineering or masterplanning capability — WSP owns that and it is not ours.

4. Who reads it

A developer, landlord or investor with a site or a scheme in trouble, usually before the money is committed. They arrive asking a version of: can this thing work, and who tells me the truth about it?

Their worries, in order: paying for a study that only says what they hoped; being handed juniors after meeting the founder; an adviser who is really a broker chasing the leasing commission; a report that stops at the pretty drawing and never names a tenant.

The page should answer all four without ever naming them as objections.

5. Voice

Take the tone straight from the homepage prototype at Web Design Projects/Husband Retail Homepage/index.html.

Premium, understated, confident. British spelling. Formal but warm. Quietly authoritative. Never salesy. Short sentences. Concrete over abstract — "we lifted footfall at X by Y%" beats "we improve performance".

Banned: growth-agency language, exclamation marks, "unlock", "elevate", "seamless", "cutting-edge", "world-class", "leverage", stacked adjectives, rhetorical questions as headings, and any superlative we cannot evidence.

6. Structure and budget

Total visible copy: 850–1,100 words. One idea per section. Every heading must tell a skimmer what that section is about — no teasers.

  1. Hero — H1 carrying the search phrase naturally, one sentence of what we do and for whom, one call to action. Left column copy, right column the enquiry form (house standard).
  2. The problem this service solves — 80–120 words, plain.
  3. What development consultancy covers here — 4–6 short blocks: concept and positioning, feasibility, tenant and brand mix, phasing, leasing readiness. Each one names the actual deliverable the client receives.
  4. How an engagement runs — the sequence, first call to handover. Say who does the work.
  5. Proof — two or three named schemes from the approved list, each with what the brief was, what Husband Retail did, what changed. Numbers only if evidenced.
  6. Why an owner picks a boutique over a giant — the honest comparison from §3.
  7. Questions people ask — 5–7, written from the real search results and People Also Ask, answered in 40–60 words each.
  8. Close — the second enquiry form and a plain invitation.

Internal links: /feasibility (once built), /leasing, the relevant project case studies, /paul.

7. House rules that apply

  • Plain language, no jargon — a fifteen-year-old must follow it.
  • Low cognitive load copy and design standards (skills low-cognitive-load-copy, low-cognitive-load-design).
  • Human-sounding copy: skill web-copy-craft — structural tells, not word lists.
  • Hero is the first two thirds of the screen, not the whole screen.
  • Two enquiry forms only: hero right-hand column and page foot. Phone required, email optional. Consent field present.
  • No pricing anywhere. No invented statistics. No claims from dossier §11.
  • The page goes through the pre-build check (skill seo-page-qa-gate) before it is called done.
  • Written with Fable 5 at very high thinking effort — announce the switch.

8. Output

Two files in Husband Retail/SEO/:

  • service-01-development-consultancy-copy.md — the copy alone, ready to drop in.
  • service-01-development-consultancy-wireframe.html — the same working template used for kw01: editable copy boxes, guidance beside them, autosave, a Wireframe tab and a Final Page tab. Reuse kw01-shopping-mall-investment-advisory-apac-wireframe-lowfi.html, do not rebuild it.

Label it MID-FI on the page. Mobile layout must be a real mobile layout.

9. The four open inputs

None of these stop the draft. Three are handled by writing honestly around them; one is a research task.

  1. Closed 10 August 2026. Volume and competition were pulled cleanly from the research tool in the logged-in browser. The stale-password diagnosis was wrong — the failures came from the dead .com.au login address, not the credential. The result is bad news: the mall-consultancy phrase family is far below the measurement floor almost everywhere (see Website/01a-serp-content-standard-feasibility.md §0). Every term in the 20-term list needs re-checking against demand before any of it is written.
  2. Is the firm still actively trading in 2026? The old site is frozen at © 2020. Confirm before publishing anything in the present tense about current mandates.
  3. Project numbers are single-sourced. Footfall, square footage, "4×/5×" at Covent Garden and the opening dates all come from Husband Retail's own old site. Either evidence them elsewhere or write the proof section without figures. Do not repeat a superlative we cannot stand behind.
  4. One short client list at the foot of the draft — the handful of things only Paul can answer, gathered in one place, nothing else asked of him.

10. The prompt to run

Write the full page copy for Husband Retail's Development Consultancy service page, page address /development-consultancy, targeting the search phrase "shopping mall concept development consultant". Read first, in this order: this document; the brand dossier at Husband Retail/knowledge-base/husband-retail-dossier.md (sections 7, 10 and 11 are binding); the search term verification at Husband Retail/SEO/husband-keyword-verification-2026-08-04.md; the homepage prototype at Web Design Projects/Husband Retail Homepage/index.html for voice; and the kw01 brief for the house brief format. Husband Retail is a Hong Kong retail real-estate consultancy, founded 1998, advising developers, landlords and investors on shopping malls and luxury retail destinations. Its buyers are institutions, never consumers. Follow sections 3 to 7 of this document exactly: the angle, the reader, the voice, the structure and word budget, and the house rules. Claim only what dossier section 10 permits. Claim nothing listed in section 11. Invent no numbers, no clients, no dates, no prices. You must be able to write the whole page from the files above without asking Paul Husband anything. Where a genuine client input is missing, leave the sentence out and add the item to a single short list at the end of the draft titled "What we still need from Paul". Deliver the two files named in section 8.

Where this came from: Ads/google-ads-strategy-2026-08-12.md is the full internal working and Ads/00-paid-search-test.md is the shorter client-facing version. This tab is the record of both, kept here so the repository holds every measured fact in one place. No new research was done for it. Every figure below is the same evidence the market tabs are built on, read as an advertising question instead of a website question — which changes several answers.

1. Why this tab exists

Building pages answers the question "does anybody who hires a firm like this one look for it on Google?" in about eighteen months. Advertising answers the same question in six weeks.

That is the whole case for it. The research in this repository has measured 243 phrases and read 108 results pages, and it has not spoken to one buyer. The Fit audit's surviving objection — that nobody has tested whether these searchers turn into clients — is not answerable by more measurement. It is answerable by putting adverts in front of the searchers and seeing who rings.

2. The budget

Per month For how long Media total
Minimum worth doing£1,2003 months£3,600
Ideal£2,5006 months£15,000
Ceiling — do not exceed£3,000——

Why there is a ceiling

This is the finding that matters most, and it runs the opposite way to most clients. The market caps the spend, not the budget.

Add up every term worth testing across all four countries and it comes to roughly 10,000 searches a month. An advert can realistically appear on maybe half of them, and about one in ten of those people click. That is 450–500 clicks a month — the entire market. At £5–6 a click, £2,900 a month is the ceiling.

Spend past it and Google does not find extra buyers, because there are none. It widens the net to phrases nobody vetted — the job boards, the dictionaries, the students, the residential buyers catalogued across the market tabs — and the test stops answering the question it was set up to answer. A bigger budget makes the result less readable, not more.

The click figures are modelled from measured search volumes. They are not observed. Week one of live spend replaces them.

Where the money goes at £2,500

Cluster Per month Clicks
A — Tenant representation (US, UK, AU)£750~150
B — Feasibility & site selection£350~70
B2 — Trade area analysis, capped on its own£150~25
C — Property & asset management (UK + AU)£920~230
D — Gulf probe£250~40

The £1,200 version keeps A and B, caps B2 at £75, and drops C and the Gulf.

3. The clusters

Cluster A — Tenant representation

The strongest test on the project. In all four markets the results page is clean: no job boards, no dictionaries, no students. Every result is a firm selling the service. Unavailable until Paul answered D7 yes on 12 August 2026.

Term Market Searches a month Cost per click Competition
tenant representationUS1,300$4.074
tenant representation servicesUS390—11
tenant rep brokerUS320$4.3715
retail tenant representationUS260$5.3416
landlord representationUS480$4.591
tenant representationUK140£11.4112
tenant representationAU170—8

£11.41 in Britain is the highest price anywhere in this repository. Nobody pays that for browsers. landlord representation scores 1 out of 100 for competition and still carries a $4.59 click — cheap to rank for and paid for at the same time, a combination that appears nowhere else in four markets.

Cluster B — Feasibility and site selection

The safest match to what the firm actually does — pillar one, in the firm's own words, and the market uses roughly the same words back.

Term Searches a month Cost per click Competition
real estate feasibility study670$4.078
retail site selection580$5.7215
trade area analysis530$52.755
site selection analysis350$13.7810
catchment area analysis330——
retail expansion strategy280$7.862

$52.75 is the highest figure in the whole repository, on a term with a competition score of 5. Somebody — almost certainly the location-data software firms — has worked out exactly what that reader is worth. Cap it separately at £150 a month or one term swallows the test. Note the competition scores in this cluster: 8, 5, 2. Everything here is worth an unpaid page as well.

Cluster C — Property and asset management

Biggest pool, riskiest read. UK 2,660 searches a month across five terms, Australia 2,530 across seven. Australia's city pages are unusually good value — real demand, competition in the teens, advertisers paying A$3–5.

⚠️ The care point, now evidenced rather than suspected. Those results pages sell hands-on management — rent collection, arrears, service charges, maintenance. The firm advises; it does not run buildings. Four further British management pages read on the afternoon of 12 August came back the same way, one of them ranking Samsic, a facilities and cleaning group. When a cleaning contractor ranks on your search term, the searcher is not looking for advice.

If that gap is not handled the enquiries will be for work the firm does not perform, and the test will read as "search doesn't work for us" when the truth is "we advertised the wrong service". That is the most likely way this whole programme produces a false negative. Either the advert headline says advisory so the click self-selects, or cluster C waits for round two.

Cluster D — the Gulf probe

The target account list is heavily Gulf — Majid Al Futtaim, Emaar, Dubai Holding, Diriyah — and the Gulf was never researched. The only figure that exists is the UAE showing about 870 searches a month, with no competition scores, no results pages and no prices.

That is normally a hard stop. Advertising is the one instrument that can answer it without a research programme behind it: £250 a month against Dubai and Riyadh for six weeks buys an answer a full sitemap exercise would take months to reach.

Conditions: it is labelled a probe, not a campaign; it is budgeted separately so it cannot cannibalise the clusters; and the results-page readings still happen before any page is built off it. A probe is permission to test, not permission to skip the gate.

The one term that fits nowhere else

cam reconciliation — 590 a month in America, competition 18, $2.90 a click, eight paid adverts. Nine well-known American lease phrases carry 21,000 searches a month between them and eight of the nine show zero advertisers. This one shows eight, and a first page of firms selling the work. That is the difference between a word people look up and a problem people pay to solve.

4. What not to put a penny on

Every item here was measured, read, and rejected for a stated reason. The list is worth as much as the buy list.

Group Terms Why
Valuationcommercial property valuation (UK 1,300), commercial property surveyor (UK 480), retail property valuation (AU 480)A Red Book valuation may only be signed by an RICS Registered Valuer — a statutory register, not a preference. Advertising a service the firm cannot legally sign generates complaints, not clients. D9 is answered yes but its condition — naming a certified valuer — is unmet

gross leasable area at 35,830 searches a month worldwide is the biggest number in this entire repository and is worth nothing in paid. Eight definitions, no sellers. A good unpaid page and a terrible advert.

The sharpest illustration of demand versus fit: commercial property management is the biggest term in the research at 11,090 a month worldwide. In the Hong Kong database it returns 20, and the tool holds no Hong Kong results page for it at all. It was originally rejected in Hong Kong because the firm did not sell the service — a reason Paul voided on 12 August. It stays rejected anyway, on demand rather than on fit. The reason changed; the answer did not.

5. Two things worth reading twice

A clean results page is not the same as a right results page. commercial property agency has a competition score of 18, twelve of twelve genuine property firms on the page, no job boards, no dictionaries, no directories — the cleanest of the four British terms read on 12 August. And it is still the wrong search, because everyone on that page is helping somebody let or buy a building. Nobody on it is being paid to advise on one.

A cost per click is a band, not a number. Two pulls of the same British terms on the same day returned £3.33 and £2.47 for commercial property agents, and £3.96 and £2.94 for commercial property management companies — a gap of about 25%. Neither is wrong. It is a rolling average of a shifting set of advertisers, not a quoted price. On a different project the same terms moved 126% in eleven days. Quote a range. Never quote a point figure, and never quote one older than a fortnight.

6. Where competitors already spend

Search volume says how many people look. Advert count says how many firms have already decided the reader is worth paying for. Where the two agree, test. Where volume is high and advert count is zero, walk away.

Advert blocks Term Market
8retail planning consultantUK — the most on any British page read
8commercial asset managementUK

8retail advisoryAU — on only 40 searches a month
8sale leaseback, retail consulting, retail strategyUS

3commercial property surveyor · property asset managementUK · AU
2most clean UK and AU service termsUK, AU
0eight of nine US lease termsUS

retail planning consultant is where paid and unpaid search agree. 140 searches a month, competition 7 — the lowest score in any of the four markets — eight advertisers already paying, and every result on the page selling the service. It is a British specialism no firm of this size covers properly. Too small for its own cluster; it belongs in the buy at £150–200 a month, and it should be the first unpaid page written.

7. How likely is it to produce a client

Money at risk Chance of at least one client Realistic range
Minimum test — £3,600~33%14% – 62%
Ideal test — £15,000~83%49% – 99%

Built from three filters, and the weakest one drives the answer:

Step Rate used Where it comes from
Clicks → enquiries1% – 2%Normal band for specialist business-to-business advisory. Assumed, not measured
Enquiries → the right kind of buyer25% – 40%Deliberately below normal, because the Fit audit says the offer may not match
Right buyer → signed work10% – 20%Typical for high-value consultancy against a shortlist

600 clicks → 6–12 enquiries → 2–5 real prospects → 0.4 clients expected → 33%. 2,700 clicks → 27–54 enquiries → 7–22 prospects → 1.8 expected → 83%.

(With an expected number of clients λ, the chance of at least one is 1 − e^−λ.)

If the true enquiry rate is 0.5% rather than 1–2%, the ideal test's 83% falls to roughly 55%. That single assumption is the most load-bearing number in this tab.

What it returns

If the first job is worth Small test Big test
£100,000 — Paul's own figure, priced 12 Aug 2026£40,000£180,000
£50,000 — a large report£20,000£90,000
£25,000 — a single study£10,000£45,000

The bottom two rows are Digital Movement's estimate, not from the client's files. A first engagement won from a cold enquiry is far more likely to be a feasibility study or a single report than a full project on day one.

The sentence that should be said out loud

A one-in-three chance means the most likely single outcome of the small test is nothing signed. Two times out of three it returns zero clients. That is still a good bet — £3,600 against a £100,000 job — but it is not a good outcome most of the time, and saying so upfront is the difference between a client who understands the test and a client who feels misled in month four.

And a £100,000 advisory engagement takes months to close. A six-month test most likely ends with pipeline, not revenue. Judge it on who rang and what they asked for, not on what was banked.

One more piece of arithmetic for the same conversation: competitors pay £11.41 a click on British tenant representation. A thousand clicks costs £11,410 — one ninth of a single project. The firms bidding that price have already done this sum.

8. What must be fixed before a penny is spent

  1. The security certificate is broken. Google can refuse to run adverts at all for a destination that will not load securely, and where it does run them, visitors see a browser warning before they see the page. Every pound spent before this is fixed is wasted.
  2. Three of the four service pages still carry Lorem ipsum. Paid traffic landing on placeholder text does more damage than no traffic, and it is grounds for a policy rejection.
  3. Three or four landing pages, one per cluster. Sending paid traffic to the homepage wastes the test.
  4. A form that records which search produced the enquiry. Without it the whole exercise is unreadable — you will know six people rang and nothing about which £920 produced them.
  5. Settle the cluster C wording question before launch, not after the first fortnight of data.

9. How to read the test while it runs

When What to look at What it tells you
End of week 1Real cost per click against the modelled £5–6The biggest source of error in this tab. If clicks cost £9, the whole plan halves
End of week 2The search terms report — the phrases people actually typedWhere you catch Google widening into jobs, students and residential
End of month 1Click-through rate per clusterA cluster below 2% is talking to the wrong people. Watch C
End of month 2First enquiries, and what they asked forThe Fit audit's question answered by real humans. Worth more than the click data
End of month 3Enquiries per 100 clicksReplaces the assumed 1–2% and the whole forecast can be rebuilt
End of month 6Pipeline, not revenueSee §7

Pruning the search terms report is a weekly job, not a setup job. Given how many of these phrases mean something else — flood risk, telecoms, aid work, stock broking, car leases, cleaning contracts, travel agents, recruitment — budget an hour a week for the first two months.

10. What this tab does not claim

  • It does not claim the offer is right. That is still open, and the Fit audit's surviving objection stands: no buyer has ever been interviewed. Paid search is a cheap way to test that. It is not a substitute for asking one.
  • It does not claim the click model is observed. The 450–500 clicks a month in §2 is arithmetic on measured volumes.
  • It does not claim any price is a quote. See §5.
  • It does not claim Hong Kong can be tested this way. On the search evidence the home market has no consultancy demand at all, and advertising will not manufacture any. Everything here points at the United States, Britain, Australia and — speculatively — the Gulf.
